\section{Chapter 04. AI arrives when the human raw material already
exists}\label{chapter-04.-ai-arrives-when-the-human-raw-material-already-exists}

\subsection{Abstract}\label{abstract-4}

Foundation models do not emerge from nowhere. The U.S. Once captured
human production can be recombined into proprietary weights, the
surveillance economy acquires a new layer.

\subsection{Keywords}\label{keywords-4}

AI arrives when the human raw material already exists; surveillance
capitalism; AI imperialism; AI weights; patrimony of humanity;
evidence-based dossier

\subsection{Research Question}\label{research-question-4}

How does ai arrives when the human raw material already exists function
within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary
record allow this chapter to establish?

\subsection{Scope and Framing Note}\label{scope-and-framing-note-4}

This paper examines ai arrives when the human raw material already
exists within the broader dossier \emph{From Surveillance Capitalism to
AI Imperialism}. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own:
necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit,
and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than
delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper
that provides the central evidentiary basis for treating frontier
weights as a patrimony and governance problem rather than a neutral
market outcome. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 00, Chapter
05, Chapter 06, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive
rather than required for basic comprehension.

\subsection{Central Thesis}\label{central-thesis-4}

Foundation models do not emerge from nowhere. They emerge after a long
period in which human expression, cultural production, and socially
filtered judgment were captured at scale, weakly compensated if at all,
and converted into machine-trainable inputs. \footnote{Sources: U.S.
  Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A1}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf}{Language
  Models are Unsupervised Mult\ldots{}} (2019-02-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix
  A2}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Evidence and Method Note}\label{evidence-and-method-note-4}

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 04 and is
grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source
notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already
normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are
asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are
marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and
speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-4}

This paper asks: How does ai arrives when the human raw material already
exists function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the
documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer
is clear from the start: Foundation models do not emerge from nowhere.
They emerge after a long period in which human expression, cultural
production, and socially filtered judgment were captured at scale,
weakly compensated if at all, and converted into machine-trainable
inputs. \footnote{Sources: U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A1}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf}{Language
  Models are Unsupervised Mult\ldots{}} (2019-02-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix
  A2}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A3})} The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show
why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument
about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

\subsection{Historical or Institutional
Context}\label{historical-or-institutional-context-4}

The U.S. Copyright Office has already treated AI training on copyrighted
works as a major live policy problem rather than a settled technical
inevitability. The 2023 inquiry and 2025 report together show that
training-input legality, disclosure, and compensation were contested
near the start of the generative-AI boom and remain contested now.
\footnote{Sources: GovInfo,
  \href{https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-08-30/pdf/2023-18624.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2023-08-30; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a4-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-notice-of-inquiry]{Appendix
  A4}); U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A1})}

\subsection{Main Analysis}\label{main-analysis-4}

The technical and legal record also undermines the industry's preferred
abstraction. Research on memorization shows that large language models
can reproduce identifiable material from training data, while court
records and legal scholarship now separate at least two layers of
conflict: training-use doctrine and input-acquisition legality.
\footnote{Sources: arXiv,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.07805}{Extracting Training Data from
  Large L\ldots{}} (2020-12-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a5-extracting-training-data-from-large-language-models]{Appendix
  A5}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15858}{Generative AI
  Training and Copyright Law} (2025-02-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a6-generative-ai-training-and-copyright-law]{Appendix
  A6}); U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California via
  CourtListener,
  \href{https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.cand.434709/gov.uscourts.cand.434709.231.0_3.pdf}{Bartz
  et al.~v. Anthropic PBC, Order\ldots{}} (2025-06-23; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a7-bartz-et-al-v-anthropic-pbc-order-on-fair-use]{Appendix
  A7}); U.S. District Court for the Southern District of New York via
  Justia,
  \href{https://cases.justia.com/federal/district-courts/new-york/nysdce/1\%3A2023cv11195/612697/514/0.pdf?ts=1743868265}{The
  New York Times Company v. Microso\ldots{}} (2025-04-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a8-the-new-york-times-company-v-microsoft-corporation-et-al-opinion-on-motions-to-dismiss]{Appendix
  A8})}

The social-to-model bridge is also explicit. GPT-2's WebText was built
from socially filtered outbound links shared on Reddit, and by February
2024 Reddit had moved from diffuse public conversation to direct
licensing, striking a major agreement that allowed Google to use Reddit
posts for AI training and related product improvement. The extraction
economy did not end with unpaid capture; it evolved into direct
licensing over accumulated human expression. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf}{Language
  Models are Unsupervised Mult\ldots{}} (2019-02-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix
  A2}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A3})}

The labor layer matters too. Research on annotation and outsourced human
labor shows that model building depends not only on scraped creative
works but also on significant human verification, correction, and
operational support that is often geographically externalized and weakly
valued. \footnote{Sources: Big Data \& Society,
  \href{https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/20539517231188723}{The
  problem with annotation. Human la\ldots{}} (2023-07-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a9-the-problem-with-annotation-human-labour-and-outsourcing-between-france-and-madagascar]{Appendix
  A9})}

Taken together, the record shows a sequence in which collectively
produced expression becomes training input, archived conversation
becomes licensable model fuel, and additional human labor is used to
clean, annotate, and operationalize the resulting systems. Value that
first appeared as diffuse public production is progressively reorganized
inside proprietary model pipelines. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf}{Language
  Models are Unsupervised Mult\ldots{}} (2019-02-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix
  A2}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A3}); Big Data \& Society,
  \href{https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/20539517231188723}{The
  problem with annotation. Human la\ldots{}} (2023-07-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a9-the-problem-with-annotation-human-labour-and-outsourcing-between-france-and-madagascar]{Appendix
  A9})}

Interpretation: the strategic move is not only model creation but
control of the interface through which machine-packaged intelligence is
accessed. In that reading, the shelf is the interface: the prompt box,
chatbot, or subscription layer becomes the first point of contact
between a buyer and capability distilled from human work. Once that
first contact is captured, value can be repriced downward and dependence
can deepen even where total technical replacement remains incomplete.
\footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf}{Language
  Models are Unsupervised Mult\ldots{}} (2019-02-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix
  A2}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A3}); Big Data \& Society,
  \href{https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/20539517231188723}{The
  problem with annotation. Human la\ldots{}} (2023-07-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a9-the-problem-with-annotation-human-labour-and-outsourcing-between-france-and-madagascar]{Appendix
  A9})}

The House AI Task Force report gives this chapter an institutional
bridge beyond agencies and courts. By December 17, 2024, a bipartisan
congressional report was already recording the training conflict in
language overlapping with the dossier's core concerns about
commercialization, consent, credit, compensation, and derivative-work
theory. \footnote{Sources: U.S. House of Representatives,
  \href{https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf}{Bipartisan
  House Task Force Report on\ldots{}} (2024-12-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a10-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A10}); TwinChat,
  \href{https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee}{AI
  Copyright Weights: A New Frontier\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a11-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law]{Appendix
  A11})}

Interpretation: this is the dossier's primary evidentiary base for
\texttt{AI\ Weights\ as\ Patrimony\ of\ Humanity}. If frontier weights
are built from collectively produced human expression and labor, then
they cannot be treated as ordinary private assets by default. The
strongest normative direction pushes toward broad, open, utility-like
availability under public-interest rules rather than permanent private
scarcity. That is advocated doctrine, not settled law. \footnote{Sources:
  TwinChat,
  \href{https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee}{AI
  Copyright Weights: A New Frontier\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a11-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law]{Appendix
  A11}); U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A1}); U.S. House of Representatives,
  \href{https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf}{Bipartisan
  House Task Force Report on\ldots{}} (2024-12-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a10-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A10})}

\subsection{Position Within the
Series}\label{position-within-the-series-4}

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest
companions are Chapter 00 (Opening framework), Chapter 05 (Talent,
overhiring, valuation, and layoffs), Chapter 06 (AI as a dual-use
rhetorical weapon), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series logic,
Chapter 04 provides the central evidentiary basis for treating frontier
weights as a patrimony and governance problem rather than a neutral
market outcome.

\subsection{Counterarguments}\label{counterarguments-4}

The current record is strong on conflict, appropriation structure,
memorization, licensing, and the social-to-model bridge. It is still
weaker on full training-stack provenance across every frontier lab. This
chapter should therefore challenge enclosure forcefully without
pretending that every corpus is already fully mapped. \footnote{Sources:
  U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A1}); U.S. District Court for the Southern District of New York via
  Justia,
  \href{https://cases.justia.com/federal/district-courts/new-york/nysdce/1\%3A2023cv11195/612697/514/0.pdf?ts=1743868265}{The
  New York Times Company v. Microso\ldots{}} (2025-04-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a8-the-new-york-times-company-v-microsoft-corporation-et-al-opinion-on-motions-to-dismiss]{Appendix
  A8})}

\subsection{Limits of the Evidence}\label{limits-of-the-evidence-4}

The current record is strong on conflict, appropriation structure,
memorization, licensing, and the social-to-model bridge. It is still
weaker on full training-stack provenance across every frontier lab. This
chapter should therefore challenge enclosure forcefully without
pretending that every corpus is already fully mapped. \footnote{Sources:
  U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A1}); U.S. District Court for the Southern District of New York via
  Justia,
  \href{https://cases.justia.com/federal/district-courts/new-york/nysdce/1\%3A2023cv11195/612697/514/0.pdf?ts=1743868265}{The
  New York Times Company v. Microso\ldots{}} (2025-04-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a8-the-new-york-times-company-v-microsoft-corporation-et-al-opinion-on-motions-to-dismiss]{Appendix
  A8})}

\subsection{Reform Relevance}\label{reform-relevance-4}

Once captured human production can be recombined into proprietary
weights, the surveillance economy acquires a new layer. It no longer
only predicts behavior. It begins to substitute for the producers whose
accumulated traces made the system possible in the first place. That is
why the ownership question has to expand into a governance question.
\footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf}{Language
  Models are Unsupervised Mult\ldots{}} (2019-02-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix
  A2}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A3}); TwinChat,
  \href{https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee}{AI
  Copyright Weights: A New Frontier\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a11-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law]{Appendix
  A11})}

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion-4}

Once captured human production can be recombined into proprietary
weights, the surveillance economy acquires a new layer. The next
question is how that enclosure story meets labor, finance, and layoffs.
In the architecture of this series, Chapter 04 therefore functions as a
self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward
the dossier's three reforms.

\subsection{Notes}\label{notes-4}

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point
readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local
appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among
\texttt{Documented\ Fact}, \texttt{Disputed\ Fact},
\texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}, and
\texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}. In Chapter 04, those boundaries
remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix
sections that summarize claims and caution points.

\subsubsection{Appendix E1. Disputed
Matters}\label{ch-04-appendix-e1-disputed-matters}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-b33-whether-existing-copyright-and-fair-use-doctrine-can-absorb-frontier-model-traini]{Appendix
  B33}: That whether existing copyright and fair-use doctrine can absorb
  frontier-model training without consent or compensation remains
  actively contested across agencies, courts, and litigants.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Appendix E2. Interpretive
Boundaries}\label{ch-04-appendix-e2-interpretive-boundaries}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-b34-dominant-intermediaries-can-consolidate-value-and-pricing-power-by-controlling-th]{Appendix
  B34}: That dominant intermediaries can consolidate value and pricing
  power by controlling the access layer through which users reach
  collectively produced goods, even when they did not produce the
  underlying material.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-b35-the-ai-prompt-box-chatbot-or-subscription-layer-can-be-read-as-the-new-shelf-or]{Appendix
  B35}: That the AI prompt box, chatbot, or subscription layer can be
  read as the new shelf or point of sale of intelligence because it
  mediates access to capabilities condensed from collective human
  production.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-b36-frontier-ai-weights-can-be-read-as-condensed-claims-over-collectively-produced-hu]{Appendix
  B36}: That frontier AI weights can be read as condensed claims over
  collectively produced human expression and therefore justify
  patrimony-oriented governance.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Appendix E3. Speculative Narrative
Risks}\label{ch-04-appendix-e3-speculative-narrative-risks}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-b37-current-legal-uncertainty-or-congressional-uptake-already-proves-frontier-ai-weig]{Appendix
  B37}: That current legal uncertainty or congressional uptake already
  proves frontier AI weights are legally settled as patrimony, public
  trust property, or common heritage.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-b38-the-supermarket-shelf-analogy-is-itself-established-as-a-documented-fact-by-the]{Appendix
  B38}: That the supermarket / shelf analogy is itself established as a
  documented fact by the legal or regulatory record rather than as a
  bounded interpretation built on that record.
\end{itemize}

Flags}\label{ch-04-appendix-e4-sensitive-or-review-worthy-note-flags}

  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a7-bartz-et-al-v-anthropic-pbc-order-on-fair-use]{Appendix
  A7}. Companion note: Note N-028. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That

  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a5-extracting-training-data-from-large-language-models]{Appendix
  A5}. Companion note: Note N-029. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That

\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a6-generative-ai-training-and-copyright-law]{Appendix

  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a10-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix

\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a9-the-problem-with-annotation-human-labour-and-outsourcing-between-france-and-madagascar]{Appendix

  systems depend on significant human labor for data generation,

\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a8-the-new-york-times-company-v-microsoft-corporation-et-al-opinion-on-motions-to-dismiss]{Appendix
  A8}. Companion note: Note N-033. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That
  another federal court outside the Anthropic case treated
  training-stage copying and dataset assembly as central issues serious
  enough to survive dismissal.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix

  major OpenAI training corpus was explicitly built from socially

\item
  \hyperref[ch-04-appendix-a11-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law]{Appendix

  A1}. Companion note: Note N-038. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That
  the U.S. Copyright Office treats AI training on copyrighted works as a

\section{Chapter 05. Talent, overhiring, valuation, and
layoffs}\label{chapter-05.-talent-overhiring-valuation-and-layoffs}

\subsection{Abstract}\label{abstract-5}

The cleanest version of the layoffs story is not that AI suddenly made
technical labor obsolete. Section 174 is a real structural factor. If
this reading holds, then the AI boom is also a class story.

\subsection{Keywords}\label{keywords-5}

Talent, overhiring, valuation, and layoffs; surveillance capitalism; AI
imperialism; evidence-based dossier

\subsection{Research Question}\label{research-question-5}

How does talent, overhiring, valuation, and layoffs function within the
dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow
this chapter to establish?

\subsection{Scope and Framing Note}\label{scope-and-framing-note-5}

This paper examines talent, overhiring, valuation, and layoffs within
the broader dossier \emph{From Surveillance Capitalism to AI
Imperialism}. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own:
necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit,
and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than
delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper
that connects the enclosure story to labor correction, balance-sheet
pressure, and the financial narratives that stabilized layoffs. Adjacent
reinforcement appears in Chapter 04, Chapter 06, Chapter 09, but those
cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic
comprehension.

\subsection{Central Thesis}\label{central-thesis-5}

The cleanest version of the layoffs story is not that AI suddenly made
technical labor obsolete. It is that AI arrived as the legitimizing
language for a correction that already had deeper causes: pandemic-era
overhiring, higher rates, margin pressure, weaker demand, and the tax
treatment of research and software spending. The cuts were not born from
one machine. They were narrated through one. \footnote{Sources: Internal
  Revenue Service,
  \href{https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/n-23-63.pdf}{Notice 2023-63:
  Guidance on Section 1\ldots{}} (2023-09-08; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a1-notice-2023-63-guidance-on-section-174-and-specified-research-or-experimental-expenditures]{Appendix
  A1}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/bbb2f3a09eafdfc7cda54a3b87e538ad}{Amazon,
  Salesforce jettison jobs in l\ldots{}} (2023-01-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a2-amazon-salesforce-jettison-jobs-in-latest-tech-worker-purge]{Appendix
  A2}); Meta,
  \href{https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/}{Mark
  Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp\ldots{}} (2023-03-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a3-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees]{Appendix
  A3}); Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/0b14d871-2d19-46f1-9660-5466301e8066}{AI
  exuberance masks broad weakness in\ldots{}} (2024-09-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a4-ai-exuberance-masks-broad-weakness-in-tech-sector-say-investors]{Appendix
  A4})}

\subsection{Evidence and Method Note}\label{evidence-and-method-note-5}

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 05 and is
grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source
notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already
normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are
asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are
marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and
speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-5}

This paper asks: How does talent, overhiring, valuation, and layoffs
function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the
documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer
is clear from the start: The cleanest version of the layoffs story is
not that AI suddenly made technical labor obsolete. It is that AI
arrived as the legitimizing language for a correction that already had
deeper causes: pandemic-era overhiring, higher rates, margin pressure,
weaker demand, and the tax treatment of research and software spending.
The cuts were not born from one machine. They were narrated through one.
\footnote{Sources: Internal Revenue Service,
  \href{https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/n-23-63.pdf}{Notice 2023-63:
  Guidance on Section 1\ldots{}} (2023-09-08; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a1-notice-2023-63-guidance-on-section-174-and-specified-research-or-experimental-expenditures]{Appendix
  A1}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/bbb2f3a09eafdfc7cda54a3b87e538ad}{Amazon,
  Salesforce jettison jobs in l\ldots{}} (2023-01-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a2-amazon-salesforce-jettison-jobs-in-latest-tech-worker-purge]{Appendix
  A2}); Meta,
  \href{https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/}{Mark
  Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp\ldots{}} (2023-03-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a3-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees]{Appendix
  A3}); Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/0b14d871-2d19-46f1-9660-5466301e8066}{AI
  exuberance masks broad weakness in\ldots{}} (2024-09-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a4-ai-exuberance-masks-broad-weakness-in-tech-sector-say-investors]{Appendix
  A4})} The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show
why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument
about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

\subsection{Historical or Institutional
Context}\label{historical-or-institutional-context-5}

Section \texttt{174} is a real structural factor. IRS guidance and
congressional repair efforts show that domestic research and software
expenses were caught in a more punitive amortization regime after 2021,
and Congress itself treated the burden as significant enough to attempt
reversal. This is not the whole story, but it is not rhetorical
decoration either. \footnote{Sources: Internal Revenue Service,
  \href{https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/n-23-63.pdf}{Notice 2023-63:
  Guidance on Section 1\ldots{}} (2023-09-08; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a1-notice-2023-63-guidance-on-section-174-and-specified-research-or-experimental-expenditures]{Appendix
  A1}); Congress.gov,
  \href{https://www.congress.gov/bill/118th-congress/house-bill/7024}{H.R.
  7024: Tax Relief for American Fa\ldots{}} (2024-01-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a5-h-r-7024-tax-relief-for-american-families-and-workers-act-of-2024]{Appendix
  A5}); Congress.gov / CRS,
  \href{https://www.congress.gov/bill/118th-congress/house-bill/7024}{H.R.
  7024: Tax Relief for American Fa\ldots{}} (2024-01-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a6-h-r-7024-tax-relief-for-american-families-and-workers-act-of-2024-crs-summary]{Appendix
  A6}); Congress.gov / Library of Congress,
  \href{https://www.congress.gov/bill/118th-congress/house-bill/7024/text}{H.R.
  7024 text: Deduction for domesti\ldots{}} (2024-03-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a7-h-r-7024-text-deduction-for-domestic-research-and-experimental-expenditures]{Appendix
  A7})}

\subsection{Main Analysis}\label{main-analysis-5}

The post-pandemic overhiring record is also strong. Executive statements
and major reporting across Meta, Amazon, and Salesforce show a later
turn toward efficiency, focus, and cost discipline after an earlier
period of aggressive expansion. \footnote{Sources: Meta,
  \href{https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/}{Mark
  Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp\ldots{}} (2023-03-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a3-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees]{Appendix
  A3}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/b884c9ac96be1c22f81d7243176a321d}{Meta
  posts lower Q4 profit, announces\ldots{}} (2023-02-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a8-meta-posts-lower-q4-profit-announces-huge-stock-buyback]{Appendix
  A8}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/abc3e389d97fb97c661ccb6acfcf65c7}{Meta
  posts sharp profit, revenue incr\ldots{}} (2024-02-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a9-meta-posts-sharp-profit-revenue-increase-in-q4-thanks-to-cost-cuts-and-advertising-rebound]{Appendix
  A9}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/bbb2f3a09eafdfc7cda54a3b87e538ad}{Amazon,
  Salesforce jettison jobs in l\ldots{}} (2023-01-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a2-amazon-salesforce-jettison-jobs-in-latest-tech-worker-purge]{Appendix
  A2})}

Top-tier reporting ties the broader correction to weaker demand, margin
pressure, and investor impatience, not only to AI. That matters because
it prevents a monocausal story. The financial correction was already
underway before AI became the universal explanation for it. \footnote{Sources:
  Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/0b14d871-2d19-46f1-9660-5466301e8066}{AI
  exuberance masks broad weakness in\ldots{}} (2024-09-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a4-ai-exuberance-masks-broad-weakness-in-tech-sector-say-investors]{Appendix
  A4}); Axios,
  \href{https://www.axios.com/2024/01/03/tech-layoffs-google-microsoft-employees-impact-anxiety}{Big
  Tech layoffs shattered industry,\ldots{}} (2024-01-03; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a10-big-tech-layoffs-shattered-industry-worker-confidence]{Appendix
  A10})}

The BIS annual report adds the macro-financial layer. By June 2026, a
major multilateral institution was openly warning that the AI capex race
was outrunning earnings and free cash flow, with leverage and financing
structures becoming part of the risk profile. The system was promising
abundance while leaning on increasingly fragile financial scaffolding.
\footnote{Sources: Bank for International Settlements,
  \href{https://www.bis.org/publ/arpdf/ar2026e.pdf}{Annual Economic
  Report 2026} (2026-06-28; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a11-annual-economic-report-2026]{Appendix
  A11})}

Interpretation: this is where the dossier's economic thesis sharpens. AI
did not invent the correction, but it became the language through which
it was justified. The same story that encloses captured human production
also helps reclassify labor cuts as technological destiny instead of the
outcome of financial structure, policy, and managerial excess. That
interpretation supports Chapter \texttt{04} by showing how enclosure and
labor subordination move together. \footnote{Sources: Meta,
  \href{https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/}{Mark
  Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp\ldots{}} (2023-03-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a3-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees]{Appendix
  A3}); Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/0b14d871-2d19-46f1-9660-5466301e8066}{AI
  exuberance masks broad weakness in\ldots{}} (2024-09-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a4-ai-exuberance-masks-broad-weakness-in-tech-sector-say-investors]{Appendix
  A4}); Bank for International Settlements,
  \href{https://www.bis.org/publ/arpdf/ar2026e.pdf}{Annual Economic
  Report 2026} (2026-06-28; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a11-annual-economic-report-2026]{Appendix
  A11})}

\subsection{Position Within the
Series}\label{position-within-the-series-5}

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest
companions are Chapter 04 (AI arrives when the human raw material
already exists), Chapter 06 (AI as a dual-use rhetorical weapon),
Chapter 09 (Future if no action is taken). In that series logic, Chapter
05 connects the enclosure story to labor correction, balance-sheet
pressure, and the financial narratives that stabilized layoffs.

\subsection{Counterarguments}\label{counterarguments-5}

This chapter should not imply that executives always lied when they
invoked AI. Some reallocation toward AI was clearly real. The stronger,
more defensible claim is narrower: AI provided a legitimizing narrative
layered on top of a structural correction with multiple preexisting
causes. \footnote{Sources: Meta,
  \href{https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/}{Mark
  Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp\ldots{}} (2023-03-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a3-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees]{Appendix
  A3}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/bbb2f3a09eafdfc7cda54a3b87e538ad}{Amazon,
  Salesforce jettison jobs in l\ldots{}} (2023-01-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a2-amazon-salesforce-jettison-jobs-in-latest-tech-worker-purge]{Appendix
  A2})}

\subsection{Limits of the Evidence}\label{limits-of-the-evidence-5}

This chapter should not imply that executives always lied when they
invoked AI. Some reallocation toward AI was clearly real. The stronger,
more defensible claim is narrower: AI provided a legitimizing narrative
layered on top of a structural correction with multiple preexisting
causes. \footnote{Sources: Meta,
  \href{https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/}{Mark
  Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp\ldots{}} (2023-03-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a3-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees]{Appendix
  A3}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/bbb2f3a09eafdfc7cda54a3b87e538ad}{Amazon,
  Salesforce jettison jobs in l\ldots{}} (2023-01-04; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a2-amazon-salesforce-jettison-jobs-in-latest-tech-worker-purge]{Appendix
  A2})}

\subsection{Reform Relevance}\label{reform-relevance-5}

If this reading holds, then the AI boom is also a class story. It
narrows the protected tier, weakens the technical middle class, and
presents downward pressure on labor as the rational price of the future.
That strengthens the patrimony argument: a system built from collective
human production is being enclosed while many of the humans who made it
possible are told the future no longer needs them on equal terms.
\footnote{Sources: Internal Revenue Service,
  \href{https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/n-23-63.pdf}{Notice 2023-63:
  Guidance on Section 1\ldots{}} (2023-09-08; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a1-notice-2023-63-guidance-on-section-174-and-specified-research-or-experimental-expenditures]{Appendix
  A1}); Bank for International Settlements,
  \href{https://www.bis.org/publ/arpdf/ar2026e.pdf}{Annual Economic
  Report 2026} (2026-06-28; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a11-annual-economic-report-2026]{Appendix
  A11}); TwinChat,
  \href{https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee}{AI
  Copyright Weights: A New Frontier\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a12-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law]{Appendix
  A12})}

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion-5}

If this reading holds, then the AI boom is also a class story. That
contradiction sharpens in the next chapter. In the architecture of this
series, Chapter 05 therefore functions as a self-contained argument
while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three
reforms.

\subsection{Notes}\label{notes-5}

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point
readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local
appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among
\texttt{Documented\ Fact}, \texttt{Disputed\ Fact},
\texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}, and
\texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}. In Chapter 05, those boundaries
remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix
sections that summarize claims and caution points.

  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a9-meta-posts-sharp-profit-revenue-increase-in-q4-thanks-to-cost-cuts-and-advertising-rebound]{Appendix
  A9}. Companion note: Note N-041. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That
  Meta's improved financial results were publicly tied to both cost
  cutting and layoffs, not just to a magical AI turnaround.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a10-big-tech-layoffs-shattered-industry-worker-confidence]{Appendix
  A10}. Companion note: Note N-042. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That
  the layoff cycle was large enough to materially change worker
  expectations across the sector.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a11-annual-economic-report-2026]{Appendix
  A11}. Companion note: Note N-043. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That
  a primary multilateral financial institution explicitly warned in June
  2026 that the AI capex race was outrunning earnings and free cash
  flow.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a6-h-r-7024-tax-relief-for-american-families-and-workers-act-of-2024-crs-summary]{Appendix
  A6}. Companion note: Note N-044. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That

\item
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a5-h-r-7024-tax-relief-for-american-families-and-workers-act-of-2024]{Appendix
  A5}. Companion note: Note N-045. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That
  Congress formally moved to soften or reverse the immediate impact of

\item
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a7-h-r-7024-text-deduction-for-domestic-research-and-experimental-expenditures]{Appendix
  A7}. Companion note: Note N-046. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That the
  practical Section 174 fight directly implicated software development,
  not only laboratory research.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-05-appendix-a4-ai-exuberance-masks-broad-weakness-in-tech-sector-say-investors]{Appendix

  A1}. Companion note: Note N-048. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That

  A3}. Companion note: Note N-049. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That

\section{Chapter 06. AI as a dual-use rhetorical
weapon}\label{chapter-06.-ai-as-a-dual-use-rhetorical-weapon}

\subsection{Abstract}\label{abstract-6}

One of the clearest contradictions in the current AI regime is that
systems are marketed simultaneously as dangerous enough to justify elite
governance and safe enough to be rolled out as broad dependence
infrastructure. OpenAI publicly framed reasoning as a distinct
capability layer tied to additional train-time and test-time compute,
and paired it with preparedness, safety, and misuse-risk language. This
is how AI becomes a dual-use rhetorical weapon.

\subsection{Keywords}\label{keywords-6}

AI as a dual-use rhetorical weapon; surveillance capitalism; AI
imperialism; evidence-based dossier

\subsection{Research Question}\label{research-question-6}

How does ai as a dual-use rhetorical weapon function within the
dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow
this chapter to establish?

\subsection{Scope and Framing Note}\label{scope-and-framing-note-6}

This paper examines ai as a dual-use rhetorical weapon within the
broader dossier \emph{From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism}.
It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context
is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material
is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo
navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that shows how
danger rhetoric, cheapness rhetoric, and reasoning rhetoric together
normalize frontier enclosure and strategic dependence. Adjacent
reinforcement appears in Chapter 04, Chapter 05, Chapter 07, Chapter 10,
but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic
comprehension.

\subsection{Central Thesis}\label{central-thesis-6}

One of the clearest contradictions in the current AI regime is that
systems are marketed simultaneously as dangerous enough to justify elite
governance and safe enough to be rolled out as broad dependence
infrastructure. The point is not that reasoning advances are fake. The
point is that real advances are being packaged inside a politics of
fear, prestige, and access management at the same time. \footnote{Sources:
  OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/learning-to-reason-with-llms/}{Learning
  to reason with LLMs} (2024-09-12; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a1-learning-to-reason-with-llms]{Appendix
  A1}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-o1-system-card/}{OpenAI o1
  System Card} (2024-12-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a2-openai-o1-system-card]{Appendix A2});
  Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/37ba7236-2a64-4807-b1e1-7e21ee7d0914}{OpenAI
  acknowledges new models increa\ldots{}} (2024-09-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a3-openai-acknowledges-new-models-increase-risk-of-misuse-to-create-bioweapons]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Evidence and Method Note}\label{evidence-and-method-note-6}

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 06 and is
grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source
notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already
normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are
asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are
marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and
speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-6}

This paper asks: How does ai as a dual-use rhetorical weapon function
within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary
record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from
the start: One of the clearest contradictions in the current AI regime
is that systems are marketed simultaneously as dangerous enough to
justify elite governance and safe enough to be rolled out as broad
dependence infrastructure. The point is not that reasoning advances are
fake. The point is that real advances are being packaged inside a
politics of fear, prestige, and access management at the same time.
\footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/learning-to-reason-with-llms/}{Learning
  to reason with LLMs} (2024-09-12; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a1-learning-to-reason-with-llms]{Appendix
  A1}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-o1-system-card/}{OpenAI o1
  System Card} (2024-12-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a2-openai-o1-system-card]{Appendix A2});
  Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/37ba7236-2a64-4807-b1e1-7e21ee7d0914}{OpenAI
  acknowledges new models increa\ldots{}} (2024-09-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a3-openai-acknowledges-new-models-increase-risk-of-misuse-to-create-bioweapons]{Appendix
  A3})} The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show
why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument
about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

\subsection{Historical or Institutional
Context}\label{historical-or-institutional-context-6}

OpenAI publicly framed reasoning as a distinct capability layer tied to
additional train-time and test-time compute, and paired it with
preparedness, safety, and misuse-risk language. The product story and
the security story arrived together. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/learning-to-reason-with-llms/}{Learning
  to reason with LLMs} (2024-09-12; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a1-learning-to-reason-with-llms]{Appendix
  A1}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-o1-system-card/}{OpenAI o1
  System Card} (2024-12-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a2-openai-o1-system-card]{Appendix A2});
  Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/37ba7236-2a64-4807-b1e1-7e21ee7d0914}{OpenAI
  acknowledges new models increa\ldots{}} (2024-09-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a3-openai-acknowledges-new-models-increase-risk-of-misuse-to-create-bioweapons]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Main Analysis}\label{main-analysis-6}

The Reuters Q* and \texttt{Strawberry} reporting shows that this
reasoning story has a serious public prehistory rather than being a
later marketing improvisation. That matters because it confirms both
sides of the contradiction: the capability arc may be real, but so is
the later struggle over how it is narrated, priced, and gated.
\footnote{Sources: Reuters,
  \href{https://www.reuters.com/technology/sam-altmans-ouster-openai-was-precipitated-by-letter-board-about-ai-breakthrough-2023-11-22/}{OpenAI
  researchers warned board of AI\ldots{}} (2023-11-23; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a4-openai-researchers-warned-board-of-ai-breakthrough-ahead-of-ceo-ouster-sources-say]{Appendix
  A4}); Reuters,
  \href{https://www.reuters.com/technology/artificial-intelligence/openai-working-new-reasoning-technology-under-code-name-strawberry-2024-07-12/}{Exclusive:
  OpenAI working on new reas\ldots{}} (2024-07-15; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a5-exclusive-openai-working-on-new-reasoning-technology-under-code-name-strawberry]{Appendix
  A5})}

Primary research strengthens the middle claim. Test-time compute is not
just a corporate slogan. A 2024 paper on compute-optimal inference-time
scaling showed meaningful efficiency improvements over simpler
baselines, while later research and open-replication efforts showed both
rapid diffusion pressure and an active technical dispute over what those
replications actually reproduced. In other words, the dossier no longer
needs to pretend that reasoning scarcity is fake in order to criticize
how it is governed. \footnote{Sources: Charlie Snell; Jaehoon Lee;
  Kelvin Xu; Aviral Kumar,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314}{Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute
  Optimal\ldots{}} (2024-08-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a6-scaling-llm-test-time-compute-optimally-can-be-more-effective-than-scaling-model-parameters]{Appendix
  A6}); Niklas Muennighoff; Zitong Yang; Weijia Shi; Xiang Lisa Li; Li
  Fei-Fei; Hannaneh Hajishirzi; Luke Zettlemoyer; Percy Liang; Emmanuel
  Candès; Tatsunori Hashimoto,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19393}{s1: Simple test-time scaling}
  (2025-01-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a7-s1-simple-test-time-scaling]{Appendix
  A7}); Guojun Wu, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14419}{It's Not That
  Simple. An Analysis of\ldots{}} (2025-07-19; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a8-it-s-not-that-simple-an-analysis-of-simple-test-time-scaling]{Appendix
  A8})}

The pricing layer is also direct now. OpenAI's own plan and API pages,
along with Anthropic's pricing and \texttt{Claude\ 4} launch material,
show that stronger reasoning access is tiered across premium plans,
higher usage classes, and higher-priced API lanes. Reasoning is not only
a capability. It is also a gated commercial product. \footnote{Sources:
  OpenAI, \href{https://chatgpt.com/pricing/}{ChatGPT Plans \textbar{}
  Free, Go, Plus, Pro,\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a9-chatgpt-plans-free-go-plus-pro-business-and-enterprise]{Appendix
  A9}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing}{Pricing
  \textbar{} OpenAI API} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a10-pricing-openai-api]{Appendix A10});
  Anthropic, \href{https://claude.com/pricing}{Plans \& Pricing
  \textbar{} Claude by Anthropic} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a11-plans-pricing-claude-by-anthropic]{Appendix
  A11}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-4}{Introducing Claude 4}
  (2025-05-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a12-introducing-claude-4]{Appendix A12})}

DeepSeek complicates total exclusivity claims without eliminating
concentration. Its releases show that parts of reasoning capability
diffuse faster than absolutist frontier rhetoric suggests. The real
story is not zero diffusion versus perfect monopoly. It is diffusion at
the edges and political curation at the core. \footnote{Sources: arXiv,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19437}{DeepSeek-V3 Technical Report}
  (2024-12-27; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a13-deepseek-v3-technical-report]{Appendix
  A13}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948}{DeepSeek-R1:
  Incentivizing Reasoning\ldots{}} (2025-01-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a14-deepseek-r1-incentivizing-reasoning-capability-in-llms-via-reinforcement-learning]{Appendix
  A14}); Niklas Muennighoff; Zitong Yang; Weijia Shi; Xiang Lisa Li; Li
  Fei-Fei; Hannaneh Hajishirzi; Luke Zettlemoyer; Percy Liang; Emmanuel
  Candès; Tatsunori Hashimoto,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19393}{s1: Simple test-time scaling}
  (2025-01-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a7-s1-simple-test-time-scaling]{Appendix A7})}

Interpretation: this chapter supports the patrimony argument by showing
how a real compute-intensive technical mechanism is turned into a
managed access regime. Fear legitimizes the gate, convenience fills the
funnel, and premium reasoning tiers convert collectively grounded
capability into selectively governed scarcity. \footnote{Sources:
  OpenAI, \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-o1-system-card/}{OpenAI
  o1 System Card} (2024-12-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a2-openai-o1-system-card]{Appendix A2});
  OpenAI, \href{https://chatgpt.com/pricing/}{ChatGPT Plans \textbar{}
  Free, Go, Plus, Pro,\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a9-chatgpt-plans-free-go-plus-pro-business-and-enterprise]{Appendix
  A9}); Anthropic, \href{https://claude.com/pricing}{Plans \& Pricing
  \textbar{} Claude by Anthropic} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a11-plans-pricing-claude-by-anthropic]{Appendix
  A11}); Charlie Snell; Jaehoon Lee; Kelvin Xu; Aviral Kumar,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314}{Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute
  Optimal\ldots{}} (2024-08-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a6-scaling-llm-test-time-compute-optimally-can-be-more-effective-than-scaling-model-parameters]{Appendix
  A6})}

\subsection{Position Within the
Series}\label{position-within-the-series-6}

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest
companions are Chapter 04 (AI arrives when the human raw material
already exists), Chapter 05 (Talent, overhiring, valuation, and
layoffs), Chapter 07 (Imperial control over access), Chapter 10 (What to
do). In that series logic, Chapter 06 shows how danger rhetoric,
cheapness rhetoric, and reasoning rhetoric together normalize frontier
enclosure and strategic dependence.

\subsection{Counterarguments}\label{counterarguments-6}

The chapter should not collapse three separate issues into one: the
reality of test-time compute as a technical mechanism, the open
challenge to frontier mystique, and the unresolved dispute over what
simple replications actually prove. The strongest claim is about
rhetoric, gating, and governance, not about proving that every frontier
capability turned out to be hollow. \footnote{Sources: Charlie Snell;
  Jaehoon Lee; Kelvin Xu; Aviral Kumar,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314}{Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute
  Optimal\ldots{}} (2024-08-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a6-scaling-llm-test-time-compute-optimally-can-be-more-effective-than-scaling-model-parameters]{Appendix
  A6}); Niklas Muennighoff; Zitong Yang; Weijia Shi; Xiang Lisa Li; Li
  Fei-Fei; Hannaneh Hajishirzi; Luke Zettlemoyer; Percy Liang; Emmanuel
  Candès; Tatsunori Hashimoto,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19393}{s1: Simple test-time scaling}
  (2025-01-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a7-s1-simple-test-time-scaling]{Appendix
  A7}); Guojun Wu, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14419}{It's Not That
  Simple. An Analysis of\ldots{}} (2025-07-19; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a8-it-s-not-that-simple-an-analysis-of-simple-test-time-scaling]{Appendix
  A8})}

\subsection{Limits of the Evidence}\label{limits-of-the-evidence-6}

The chapter should not collapse three separate issues into one: the
reality of test-time compute as a technical mechanism, the open
challenge to frontier mystique, and the unresolved dispute over what
simple replications actually prove. The strongest claim is about
rhetoric, gating, and governance, not about proving that every frontier
capability turned out to be hollow. \footnote{Sources: Charlie Snell;
  Jaehoon Lee; Kelvin Xu; Aviral Kumar,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314}{Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute

  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a6-scaling-llm-test-time-compute-optimally-can-be-more-effective-than-scaling-model-parameters]{Appendix
  A6}); Niklas Muennighoff; Zitong Yang; Weijia Shi; Xiang Lisa Li; Li
  Fei-Fei; Hannaneh Hajishirzi; Luke Zettlemoyer; Percy Liang; Emmanuel
  Candès; Tatsunori Hashimoto,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19393}{s1: Simple test-time scaling}
  (2025-01-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a7-s1-simple-test-time-scaling]{Appendix
  A7}); Guojun Wu, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14419}{It's Not That

  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a8-it-s-not-that-simple-an-analysis-of-simple-test-time-scaling]{Appendix
  A8})}

\subsection{Reform Relevance}\label{reform-relevance-6}

This is how AI becomes a dual-use rhetorical weapon. Danger justifies
concentration, while convenience justifies mass adoption. A public told
that the system is too dangerous to democratize and too useful to refuse
is being prepared to accept hierarchy as prudence. That pushes Chapter
\texttt{04}'s ownership question toward a sharper answer: capability
built from collective human production is being enclosed as a governed
premium. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-o1-system-card/}{OpenAI o1
  System Card} (2024-12-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a2-openai-o1-system-card]{Appendix A2});
  OpenAI, \href{https://chatgpt.com/pricing/}{ChatGPT Plans \textbar{}
  Free, Go, Plus, Pro,\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a9-chatgpt-plans-free-go-plus-pro-business-and-enterprise]{Appendix
  A9}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-4}{Introducing Claude 4}
  (2025-05-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-06-appendix-a12-introducing-claude-4]{Appendix A12})}

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion-6}

This is how AI becomes a dual-use rhetorical weapon. Once managed
dependency exists, the next question is who controls the most
consequential access lanes and under what geopolitical terms. In the
architecture of this series, Chapter 06 therefore functions as a
self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward
the dossier's three reforms.

\subsection{Notes}\label{notes-6}

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point
readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local
appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among
\texttt{Documented\ Fact}, \texttt{Disputed\ Fact},
\texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}, and
\texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}. In Chapter 06, those boundaries
remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix
sections that summarize claims and caution points.

\section{Chapter 07. Imperial control over
access}\label{chapter-07.-imperial-control-over-access}

\subsection{Abstract}\label{abstract-7}

The AI system is not simply a market of competing products. The United
States has already moved from broad leadership rhetoric into formal
doctrine, consultation processes, and implementation guidance. If
frontier AI is governed through chokepoints, then intelligence itself
becomes a stratified geopolitical infrastructure.

\subsection{Keywords}\label{keywords-7}

Imperial control over access; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism;
government capture; evidence-based dossier

\subsection{Research Question}\label{research-question-7}

How does imperial control over access function within the dossier's
larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter
to establish?

\subsection{Scope and Framing Note}\label{scope-and-framing-note-7}

This paper examines imperial control over access within the broader
dossier \emph{From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism}. It is
intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is
restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is
reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation.
It contributes to the series as a paper that establishes chokepoints,
strategic access, and state-company interfaces that justify anti-capture
obligations. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 06, Chapter 08,
Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather than
required for basic comprehension.

\subsection{Central Thesis}\label{central-thesis-7}

The AI system is not simply a market of competing products. It is also
an architecture of chokepoints. Export controls, cloud dependence, chip
access, and defense-state adjacency increasingly define who can build,
scale, or even reach frontier intelligence. The question is no longer
only who invents. It is who gets permission. \footnote{Sources: The
  White House,
  \href{https://www.whitehouse.gov/presidential-actions/2025/01/removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence/}{Removing
  Barriers to American Leaders\ldots{}} (2025-01-23; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a1-removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A1}); Bureau of Industry and Security,
  \href{https://www.bis.gov/media/documents/bis-guidance-may-31-2026.pdf}{Guidance
  Regarding Enforcement of Lic\ldots{}} (2026-05-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a2-guidance-regarding-enforcement-of-license-requirements-for-advanced-computing-items-for-entities-headquartered-in-country-group-d-5-and-macau]{Appendix
  A2}); Amazon,
  \href{https://press.aboutamazon.com/2023/9/amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai}{Amazon
  and Anthropic Announce Strateg\ldots{}} (2023-09-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a3-amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Evidence and Method Note}\label{evidence-and-method-note-7}

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 07 and is
grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source
notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already
normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are
asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are
marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and
speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-7}

This paper asks: How does imperial control over access function within
the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record
allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the
start: The AI system is not simply a market of competing products. It is
also an architecture of chokepoints. Export controls, cloud dependence,
chip access, and defense-state adjacency increasingly define who can
build, scale, or even reach frontier intelligence. The question is no
longer only who invents. It is who gets permission. \footnote{Sources:
  The White House,
  \href{https://www.whitehouse.gov/presidential-actions/2025/01/removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence/}{Removing
  Barriers to American Leaders\ldots{}} (2025-01-23; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a1-removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A1}); Bureau of Industry and Security,
  \href{https://www.bis.gov/media/documents/bis-guidance-may-31-2026.pdf}{Guidance
  Regarding Enforcement of Lic\ldots{}} (2026-05-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a2-guidance-regarding-enforcement-of-license-requirements-for-advanced-computing-items-for-entities-headquartered-in-country-group-d-5-and-macau]{Appendix
  A2}); Amazon,
  \href{https://press.aboutamazon.com/2023/9/amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai}{Amazon
  and Anthropic Announce Strateg\ldots{}} (2023-09-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a3-amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai]{Appendix
  A3})} The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show
why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument
about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

\subsection{Historical or Institutional
Context}\label{historical-or-institutional-context-7}

The United States has already moved from broad leadership rhetoric into
formal doctrine, consultation processes, and implementation guidance.
Executive action, federal requests for information, and corporate
positioning now form a visible policy sequence rather than a vague
background condition. \footnote{Sources: The White House,
  \href{https://www.whitehouse.gov/presidential-actions/2025/01/removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence/}{Removing
  Barriers to American Leaders\ldots{}} (2025-01-23; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a1-removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A1}); Federal Register,
  \href{https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2025/02/06/2025-02305/request-for-information-on-the-development-of-an-artificial-intelligence-ai-action-plan}{Request
  for Information on the Develo\ldots{}} (2025-02-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a4-request-for-information-on-the-development-of-an-artificial-intelligence-ai-action-plan]{Appendix
  A4}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/}{OpenAI's
  proposals for the U.S. AI Ac\ldots{}} (2025-03-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a5-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan]{Appendix
  A5})}

\subsection{Main Analysis}\label{main-analysis-7}

BIS guidance gives the advanced-computing export regime an operational
enforcement layer. The politics of AI access is therefore not merely
metaphorical. It is already administrative, technical, and executable.
\footnote{Sources: Bureau of Industry and Security,
  \href{https://www.bis.gov/media/documents/bis-guidance-may-31-2026.pdf}{Guidance
  Regarding Enforcement of Lic\ldots{}} (2026-05-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a2-guidance-regarding-enforcement-of-license-requirements-for-advanced-computing-items-for-entities-headquartered-in-country-group-d-5-and-macau]{Appendix
  A2})}

Hyperscaler dependence is also documented. At least one major frontier
lab publicly tied the majority of its workloads and future development
to a single cloud giant, which means frontier capability is already
routed through a small number of infrastructural landlords. \footnote{Sources:
  Amazon,
  \href{https://press.aboutamazon.com/2023/9/amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai}{Amazon
  and Anthropic Announce Strateg\ldots{}} (2023-09-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a3-amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai]{Appendix
  A3})}

The formal government-facing layer is now public as well. OpenAI's
\texttt{OpenAI\ for\ Government}, Anthropic's \texttt{Claude\ Gov}, and
the Palantir-AWS-Anthropic classified distribution lane show that
frontier access is organized not only through markets but through
accredited, restricted, and government-priority channels. \footnote{Sources:
  OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a6-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A6}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude
  Gov models for U.S. national s\ldots{}} (2025-06-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a7-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix
  A7}); Palantir Technologies,
  \href{https://investors.palantir.com/news-details/2024/Anthropic-and-Palantir-Partner-to-Bring-Claude-AI-Models-to-AWS-for-U.S.-Government-Intelligence-and-Defense-Operations/}{Anthropic
  and Palantir Partner to Bri\ldots{}} (2024-11-07; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a8-anthropic-and-palantir-partner-to-bring-claude-ai-models-to-aws-for-u-s-government-intelligence-and-defense-operations]{Appendix
  A8})}

The personnel interface is explicit too. The Army's
\texttt{Detachment\ 201} demonstrates that the boundary between senior
frontier-tech leadership and military institutions has become porous in
public view. That does not prove improper command. It does prove public
institutional integration. \footnote{Sources: U.S. Army Public Affairs,
  \href{https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation}{Army
  Launches Detachment 201: Executi\ldots{}} (2025-06-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a9-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation]{Appendix
  A9})}

In-Q-Tel, Palantir, and the Thiel-linked network provide a serious
documentary basis for talking about venture-state-security adjacency
without drifting into mythology. The structure is the point: chips,
clouds, accredited environments, contracts, and strategic interfaces
remain highly concentrated even if some model knowledge diffuses.
\footnote{Sources: In-Q-Tel, \href{https://www.iqt.org/}{In-Q-Tel}
  (n.d.; see \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a10-in-q-tel]{Appendix A10});
  Palantir, \href{https://www.palantir.com/platforms/gotham/}{Gotham}
  (n.d.; see \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a11-gotham]{Appendix A11}); SEC,
  \href{https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000132680122000043/meta2022definitiveproxysta.htm}{Meta
  Platforms, Inc.~2022 Definitive\ldots{}} (2022-04-08; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a12-meta-platforms-inc-2022-definitive-proxy-statement]{Appendix
  A12}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948}{DeepSeek-R1:
  Incentivizing Reasoning\ldots{}} (2025-01-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a13-deepseek-r1-incentivizing-reasoning-capability-in-llms-via-reinforcement-learning]{Appendix
  A13})}

Interpretation: this is where
\texttt{Limit\ Government\ Capture\ by\ AI\ Companies} begins to look
structurally necessary. Once public support, export-control protection,
privileged state access, and defense channels become material to
frontier advantage, the resulting intelligence cannot be defended as a
purely ordinary private market outcome. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a6-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A6}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude
  Gov models for U.S. national s\ldots{}} (2025-06-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a7-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix
  A7}); Bureau of Industry and Security,
  \href{https://www.bis.gov/media/documents/bis-guidance-may-31-2026.pdf}{Guidance
  Regarding Enforcement of Lic\ldots{}} (2026-05-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a2-guidance-regarding-enforcement-of-license-requirements-for-advanced-computing-items-for-entities-headquartered-in-country-group-d-5-and-macau]{Appendix
  A2})}

\subsection{Position Within the
Series}\label{position-within-the-series-7}

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest
companions are Chapter 06 (AI as a dual-use rhetorical weapon), Chapter
08 (Power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture), Chapter 10
(What to do). In that series logic, Chapter 07 establishes chokepoints,
strategic access, and state-company interfaces that justify anti-capture
obligations.

\subsection{Counterarguments}\label{counterarguments-7}

This chapter must not confuse chokepoints with total closure. The
evidence supports concentrated leverage, privileged lanes, and formal
state-company interfaces, not omnipotent control over all capability
diffusion. \footnote{Sources: arXiv,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948}{DeepSeek-R1: Incentivizing
  Reasoning\ldots{}} (2025-01-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a13-deepseek-r1-incentivizing-reasoning-capability-in-llms-via-reinforcement-learning]{Appendix
  A13}); Amazon,
  \href{https://press.aboutamazon.com/2023/9/amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai}{Amazon
  and Anthropic Announce Strateg\ldots{}} (2023-09-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a3-amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Limits of the Evidence}\label{limits-of-the-evidence-7}

This chapter must not confuse chokepoints with total closure. The
evidence supports concentrated leverage, privileged lanes, and formal
state-company interfaces, not omnipotent control over all capability
diffusion. \footnote{Sources: arXiv,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948}{DeepSeek-R1: Incentivizing
  Reasoning\ldots{}} (2025-01-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a13-deepseek-r1-incentivizing-reasoning-capability-in-llms-via-reinforcement-learning]{Appendix
  A13}); Amazon,
  \href{https://press.aboutamazon.com/2023/9/amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai}{Amazon
  and Anthropic Announce Strateg\ldots{}} (2023-09-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a3-amazon-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-collaboration-to-advance-generative-ai]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Reform Relevance}\label{reform-relevance-7}

If frontier AI is governed through chokepoints, then intelligence itself
becomes a stratified geopolitical infrastructure. Access is no longer
merely a subscription question. It becomes a question of empire, supply
chains, protected lanes, and permission. \footnote{Sources: The White
  House,
  \href{https://www.whitehouse.gov/presidential-actions/2025/01/removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence/}{Removing
  Barriers to American Leaders\ldots{}} (2025-01-23; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a1-removing-barriers-to-american-leadership-in-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A1}); Bureau of Industry and Security,
  \href{https://www.bis.gov/media/documents/bis-guidance-may-31-2026.pdf}{Guidance
  Regarding Enforcement of Lic\ldots{}} (2026-05-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a2-guidance-regarding-enforcement-of-license-requirements-for-advanced-computing-items-for-entities-headquartered-in-country-group-d-5-and-macau]{Appendix
  A2}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-07-appendix-a6-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A6})}

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion-7}

If frontier AI is governed through chokepoints, then intelligence itself
becomes a stratified geopolitical infrastructure. But chokepoints alone
do not explain public acceptance. In the architecture of this series,
Chapter 07 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also
advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

\subsection{Notes}\label{notes-7}

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point
readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local
appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among
\texttt{Documented\ Fact}, \texttt{Disputed\ Fact},
\texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}, and
\texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}. In Chapter 07, those boundaries
remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix
sections that summarize claims and caution points.
