\section{Chapter 08. Power network, legitimacy, and institutional
capture}\label{chapter-08.-power-network-legitimacy-and-institutional-capture}

\subsection{Abstract}\label{abstract-8}

Frontier firms do not consolidate power by product alone. OpenAI
publicly treated the November 2023 crisis as a governance rupture and
responded with a visible legitimacy-repair process that included board
reset, formal review, and later restructuring. Once legitimacy is
institutionally assembled, frontier AI stops looking like just another
industry and begins to look like a state-adjacent governing
infrastructure.

\subsection{Keywords}\label{keywords-8}

Power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture; surveillance
capitalism; AI imperialism; government capture; evidence-based dossier

\subsection{Research Question}\label{research-question-8}

How does power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture function
within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary
record allow this chapter to establish?

\subsection{Scope and Framing Note}\label{scope-and-framing-note-8}

This paper examines power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture
within the broader dossier \emph{From Surveillance Capitalism to AI
Imperialism}. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own:
necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit,
and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than
delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper
that shows how access is legitimized, stabilized, and normalized through
governance, lobbying, elite networks, and institutional prestige.
Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 07, Chapter 09, Chapter 10,
but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic
comprehension.

\subsection{Central Thesis}\label{central-thesis-8}

Frontier firms do not consolidate power by product alone. They do so by
building legitimacy around the product. That legitimacy is assembled
through governance form, elite board composition, security credentials,
policy access, operational state interfaces, philanthropy, and political
spending. The result is not necessarily a secret cabal. It is a
documented field of mutual reinforcement through ordinary institutions.
\footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-leadership-transition/}{OpenAI
  announces leadership transition} (2023-11-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition]{Appendix
  A1}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/6ef612a3a0fcaef05480bbd1ebbd79b1}{OpenAI
  appoints former top US cyberwa\ldots{}} (2024-06-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a2-openai-appoints-former-top-us-cyberwarrior-paul-nakasone-to-its-board-of-directors]{Appendix
  A2}); Axios,
  \href{https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter}{Anthropic
  outspends OpenAI in biggest\ldots{}} (2026-04-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Evidence and Method Note}\label{evidence-and-method-note-8}

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 08 and is
grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source
notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already
normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are
asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are
marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and
speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-8}

This paper asks: How does power network, legitimacy, and institutional
capture function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the
documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer
is clear from the start: Frontier firms do not consolidate power by
product alone. They do so by building legitimacy around the product.
That legitimacy is assembled through governance form, elite board
composition, security credentials, policy access, operational state
interfaces, philanthropy, and political spending. The result is not
necessarily a secret cabal. It is a documented field of mutual
reinforcement through ordinary institutions. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-leadership-transition/}{OpenAI
  announces leadership transition} (2023-11-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition]{Appendix
  A1}); Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/6ef612a3a0fcaef05480bbd1ebbd79b1}{OpenAI
  appoints former top US cyberwa\ldots{}} (2024-06-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a2-openai-appoints-former-top-us-cyberwarrior-paul-nakasone-to-its-board-of-directors]{Appendix
  A2}); Axios,
  \href{https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter}{Anthropic
  outspends OpenAI in biggest\ldots{}} (2026-04-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter]{Appendix
  A3})} The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show
why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument
about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

\subsection{Historical or Institutional
Context}\label{historical-or-institutional-context-8}

OpenAI publicly treated the November 2023 crisis as a governance rupture
and responded with a visible legitimacy-repair process that included
board reset, formal review, and later restructuring. That shows
legitimacy was not incidental to frontier status. It had to be actively
rebuilt. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-leadership-transition/}{OpenAI
  announces leadership transition} (2023-11-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition]{Appendix
  A1}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-new-members-to-board-of-directors/}{OpenAI
  announces new members to board\ldots{}} (2024-03-08; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a4-openai-announces-new-members-to-board-of-directors]{Appendix
  A4}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee/}{OpenAI
  board forms safety and securit\ldots{}} (2024-05-28; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a5-openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee]{Appendix
  A5})}

\subsection{Main Analysis}\label{main-analysis-8}

The company's later appointments and committee structures show
legitimacy being rebuilt through establishment credentials as much as
technical authority. Security and defense credibility became part of the
governance story. \footnote{Sources: Associated Press,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/6ef612a3a0fcaef05480bbd1ebbd79b1}{OpenAI
  appoints former top US cyberwa\ldots{}} (2024-06-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a2-openai-appoints-former-top-us-cyberwarrior-paul-nakasone-to-its-board-of-directors]{Appendix
  A2}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/index/openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee/}{OpenAI
  board forms safety and securit\ldots{}} (2024-05-28; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a5-openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee]{Appendix
  A5}); Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/63b08a9d-e537-4d60-9904-c59958a16982}{OpenAI
  appoints one of Wall Street's\ldots{}} (2025-01-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a6-openai-appoints-one-of-wall-street-s-most-powerful-dealmakers-to-its-board]{Appendix
  A6})}

The policy and state-interface layers described in Chapter \texttt{07}
matter differently here. \texttt{OpenAI\ for\ Government},
\texttt{Claude\ Gov}, classified-distribution channels, and
\texttt{Detachment\ 201} show how privileged state access can be
normalized as routine institutional partnership rather than treated as
an exceptional concentration of power. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A7}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude
  Gov models for U.S. national s\ldots{}} (2025-06-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix
  A8}); Palantir Technologies,
  \href{https://investors.palantir.com/news-details/2024/Anthropic-and-Palantir-Partner-to-Bring-Claude-AI-Models-to-AWS-for-U.S.-Government-Intelligence-and-Defense-Operations/}{Anthropic
  and Palantir Partner to Bri\ldots{}} (2024-11-07; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a9-anthropic-and-palantir-partner-to-bring-claude-ai-models-to-aws-for-u-s-government-intelligence-and-defense-operations]{Appendix
  A9}); U.S. Army Public Affairs,
  \href{https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation}{Army
  Launches Detachment 201: Executi\ldots{}} (2025-06-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a10-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation]{Appendix
  A10})}

The influence layer widened by 2026. Lobbying disclosures and PAC
records show that frontier legitimacy expanded beyond public messaging
into formal Washington and electoral infrastructure. That means
legitimacy was not only reputational. It was procedural and political.
\footnote{Sources: Axios,
  \href{https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter}{Anthropic
  outspends OpenAI in biggest\ldots{}} (2026-04-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter]{Appendix
  A3}); Federal Election Commission,
  \href{https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/}{LEADING THE
  FUTURE - committee overview} (2026-03-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a11-leading-the-future-committee-overview]{Appendix
  A11})}

The moral-authority layer widened too. Dario Amodei publicly warned that
AI-driven wealth concentration could destabilize democracy while also
describing the founders' 80\% wealth pledge. The Vatican's 2026
encyclical shows that the legitimacy field had expanded beyond industry
and policy into formal civilizational and religious authority. These
sources do not absolve frontier firms. They show how moral positioning
becomes part of the legitimacy field itself. \footnote{Sources: Dario
  Amodei,
  \href{https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology}{The
  Adolescence of Technology} (2026-01-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a12-the-adolescence-of-technology]{Appendix
  A12}); The Holy See,
  \href{https://www.vatican.va/content/leo-xiv/en/encyclicals/documents/20260515-magnifica-humanitas.html}{Encyclical
  Letter of His Holiness Leo\ldots{}} (2026-05-15; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a13-encyclical-letter-of-his-holiness-leo-xiv-magnifica-humanitas]{Appendix
  A13})}

Another part of the legitimacy problem is private survivability hedging.
In 2016, Sam Altman publicly described storing guns, gold, potassium
iodide, antibiotics, gas masks, and having a New Zealand fallback plan
tied to Peter Thiel. In 2023, Wired documented Mark Zuckerberg's Kauai
compound as including an underground shelter, secrecy controls on
construction crews, and self-sufficiency features; in 2024, Zuckerberg
publicly minimized the structure as ``just a little shelter.'' None of
this proves that these actors expect imminent collapse or are
engineering it. But it does sharpen a bounded political question:
legitimacy weakens when elites ask publics and states to normalize
dependency on systems they govern while privately investing in exemption
from shared risk. \footnote{Sources: Tad Friend,
  \href{https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2016/10/10/sam-altmans-manifest-destiny}{Sam
  Altman's Manifest Destiny} (2016-10-03; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a14-sam-altman-s-manifest-destiny]{Appendix
  A14}); Makena Kelly,
  \href{https://www.wired.com/story/mark-zuckerberg-inside-hawaii-compound}{Inside
  Mark Zuckerberg's Top-Secret H\ldots{}} (2023-12-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a15-inside-mark-zuckerbergs-top-secret-hawaii-compound]{Appendix
  A15}); Charlotte Phillipp,
  \href{https://people.com/mark-zuckerberg-hawaii-bunker-5000-square-feet-8765376}{Mark
  Zuckerberg Downplays His 5,000-S\ldots{}} (2024-12-20; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a16-mark-zuckerberg-downplays-his-5-000-square-foot-underground-bunker-in-hawaii-just-a-little-shelter]{Appendix
  A16})}

Named-individual material belongs here only when it clarifies
institutional control, mission conflict, or legal architecture. The
Musk-OpenAI litigation matters because it shows that nonprofit mission,
restructuring, and governance control became a formal legal
battleground. The AP injunction-denial reporting further shows that the
dispute remained live even after emergency relief was denied.
\footnote{Sources: CourtListener / PACER,
  \href{https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69013420/musk-v-altman/}{Musk
  v. Altman, 4:24-cv-04722} (2024-08-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a17-musk-v-altman-4-24-cv-04722]{Appendix
  A17}); Matt O'Brien; Barbara Ortutay,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/elon-musk-openai-lawsuit-f5724e7ab07b5bed8292a1e8aa2ef695}{Judge
  denies Elon Musk's request to b\ldots{}} (2025-03-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a18-judge-denies-elon-musk-s-request-to-block-openai-for-profit-conversion-but-welcomes-trial]{Appendix
  A18}); Financial Times,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/532931dd-e1c0-4f18-a8af-5777dd70a52d}{Judge
  denies Elon Musk's attempt to i\ldots{}} (2025-03-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a19-judge-denies-elon-musks-attempt-to-immediately-block-openais-conversion-to-for-profit-entity]{Appendix
  A19})}

Interpretation: this chapter builds the structural case for
\texttt{Limit\ Government\ Capture\ by\ AI\ Companies}. The strongest
reading is not hidden sovereign command. It is that governance form,
security credentials, policy access, lobbying, philanthropy, and
operational state interfaces together normalize extraordinary
concentration before democratic controls catch up. \footnote{Sources:
  OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/}{OpenAI's
  proposals for the U.S. AI Ac\ldots{}} (2025-03-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan]{Appendix
  A20}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A7}); Axios,
  \href{https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter}{Anthropic
  outspends OpenAI in biggest\ldots{}} (2026-04-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter]{Appendix
  A3}); Dario Amodei,
  \href{https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology}{The
  Adolescence of Technology} (2026-01-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a12-the-adolescence-of-technology]{Appendix
  A12})}

\subsection{Position Within the
Series}\label{position-within-the-series-8}

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest
companions are Chapter 07 (Imperial control over access), Chapter 09
(Future if no action is taken), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series
logic, Chapter 08 shows how access is legitimized, stabilized, and
normalized through governance, lobbying, elite networks, and
institutional prestige.

\subsection{Counterarguments}\label{counterarguments-8}

The chapter must preserve a crucial distinction: elite overlap and
influence channels do not by themselves prove unified hidden control.
The record is strongest on formal influence, legitimacy repair,
disclosed interfaces, and institutional reinforcement, not on secret
ownership of every outcome. \footnote{Sources: CourtListener / PACER,
  \href{https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69013420/musk-v-altman/}{Musk
  v. Altman, 4:24-cv-04722} (2024-08-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a17-musk-v-altman-4-24-cv-04722]{Appendix
  A17}); Federal Election Commission,
  \href{https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/}{LEADING THE
  FUTURE - committee overview} (2026-03-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a11-leading-the-future-committee-overview]{Appendix
  A11})}

\subsection{Limits of the Evidence}\label{limits-of-the-evidence-8}

The chapter must preserve a crucial distinction: elite overlap and
influence channels do not by themselves prove unified hidden control.
The record is strongest on formal influence, legitimacy repair,
disclosed interfaces, and institutional reinforcement, not on secret
ownership of every outcome. \footnote{Sources: CourtListener / PACER,
  \href{https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69013420/musk-v-altman/}{Musk
  v. Altman, 4:24-cv-04722} (2024-08-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a17-musk-v-altman-4-24-cv-04722]{Appendix
  A17}); Federal Election Commission,
  \href{https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/}{LEADING THE
  FUTURE - committee overview} (2026-03-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a11-leading-the-future-committee-overview]{Appendix
  A11})}

\subsection{Reform Relevance}\label{reform-relevance-8}

Once legitimacy is institutionally assembled, frontier AI stops looking
like just another industry and begins to look like a state-adjacent
governing infrastructure. That is why any material government
relationship with frontier firms should trigger transparency,
conflict-of-interest controls, auditability, procurement visibility, and
limits on exclusive private benefit. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A7}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude
  Gov models for U.S. national s\ldots{}} (2025-06-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix
  A8}); U.S. Army Public Affairs,
  \href{https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation}{Army
  Launches Detachment 201: Executi\ldots{}} (2025-06-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a10-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation]{Appendix
  A10}); Axios,
  \href{https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter}{Anthropic
  outspends OpenAI in biggest\ldots{}} (2026-04-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion-8}

Once legitimacy is institutionally assembled, frontier AI stops looking
like just another industry and begins to look like a state-adjacent
governing infrastructure. At that point the dossier can ask its final
scenario question: if the present trajectory continues, what kind of
social order does it produce? In the architecture of this series,
Chapter 08 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also
advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

\subsection{Notes}\label{notes-8}

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point
readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local
appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among
\texttt{Documented\ Fact}, \texttt{Disputed\ Fact},
\texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}, and
\texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}. In Chapter 08, those boundaries
remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix
sections that summarize claims and caution points.

\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b68-allegations-advanced-by-named-actors-in-openai-governance-disputes-are-procedural]{Appendix
  B68}: That allegations advanced by named actors in OpenAI governance
  disputes are procedurally live and politically relevant without being

\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Appendix E2. Interpretive
Boundaries}\label{ch-08-appendix-e2-interpretive-boundaries}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b69-current-official-records-show-frontier-labs-and-adjacent-platform-firms-building]{Appendix
  B69}: That current official records show frontier labs and adjacent
  platform firms building formal government-facing channels through
  secure offerings, classified-environment access, accredited cloud
  stacks, and even military personnel embedding rather than engaging the
  state only through lobbying or abstract policy rhetoric.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b70-frontier-legitimacy-is-increasingly-stabilized-through-a-combination-of-mission-r]{Appendix
  B70}: That frontier legitimacy is increasingly stabilized through a
  combination of mission rhetoric, elite board composition, security
  credentials, policy access, and political spending.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b71-the-legitimacy-field-around-frontier-ai-is-now-reinforced-not-only-by-boards-pol]{Appendix
  B71}: That the legitimacy field around frontier AI is now reinforced
  not only by boards, policy essays, and lobbying, but by operational
  state interfaces that normalize privileged access and public-private
  dependence.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b72-the-strongest-bounded-reading-of-elite-fallback-architecture-is-a-revealed-prefer]{Appendix
  B72}: That the strongest bounded reading of elite fallback
  architecture is a revealed-preference gap between public stewardship
  language and private survivability hedging.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b73-openai-s-governance-rupture-safety-rhetoric-leadership-churn-and-reasoning-mod]{Appendix
  B73}: That OpenAI's governance rupture, safety rhetoric, leadership
  churn, and reasoning-model rollout are best read as parts of one
  reorganization of authority rather than as isolated episodes.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b74-named-individual-disputes-should-enter-the-dossier-only-when-they-clarify-governa]{Appendix
  B74}: That named-individual disputes should enter the dossier only
  when they clarify governance structure, institutional control, or
  legal architecture rather than functioning as scandal collage.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Appendix E3. Speculative Narrative
Risks}\label{ch-08-appendix-e3-speculative-narrative-risks}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b75-bunker-ownership-survival-planning-or-retreat-architecture-prove-elite-foreknow]{Appendix
  B75}: That bunker ownership, survival planning, or retreat
  architecture prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or that
  frontier firms are knowingly engineering it.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b76-elite-overlap-among-frontier-labs-venture-capital-lobbying-networks-and-defens]{Appendix
  B76}: That elite overlap among frontier labs, venture capital,
  lobbying networks, and defense-adjacent institutions proves a single
  hidden command chain.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b77-founder-philanthropy-or-public-warnings-about-concentration-prove-elite-foreknowl]{Appendix
  B77}: That founder philanthropy or public warnings about concentration
  prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or absolve frontier
  firms of responsibility.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b78-formal-government-only-model-channels-classified-distribution-stacks-or-militar]{Appendix
  B78}: That formal government-only model channels, classified
  distribution stacks, or military personnel embedding by themselves
  prove unlawful capture or total hidden state control over frontier AI
  firms.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-08-appendix-b79-elite-overlap-litigation-conflict-or-reputational-controversy-around-named-indi]{Appendix
  B79}: That elite overlap, litigation conflict, or reputational
  controversy around named individuals justifies guilt by association
  without direct structural proof.
\end{itemize}

\section{Chapter 09. Future if no action is
taken}\label{chapter-09.-future-if-no-action-is-taken}

\subsection{Abstract}\label{abstract-9}

The most credible continuity scenario is not science-fiction apocalypse.
The U.S. If no action is taken, platform immunity, private control over
weights, and opaque state-company entanglement stop looking like
separate policy issues and start looking like one reinforced system.

\subsection{Keywords}\label{keywords-9}

Future if no action is taken; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism;
evidence-based dossier

\subsection{Research Question}\label{research-question-9}

How does future if no action is taken function within the dossier's
larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter
to establish?

\subsection{Scope and Framing Note}\label{scope-and-framing-note-9}

This paper examines future if no action is taken within the broader
dossier \emph{From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism}. It is
intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is
restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is
reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation.
It contributes to the series as a paper that explains why inaction would
compound dependency, hierarchy, and cognitive enclosure across the whole
system. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 05, Chapter 07,
Chapter 08, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather
than required for basic comprehension.

\subsection{Central Thesis}\label{central-thesis-9}

The most credible continuity scenario is not science-fiction apocalypse.
It is a slower regime of deepening dependence, shrinking cognitive
sovereignty, weakened middle-class bargaining power, and increasingly
hierarchical access to machine intelligence. \footnote{Sources: U.S.
  Department of Education / ERIC,
  \href{https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097}{Artificial Intelligence and
  the Futur\ldots{}} (2023-05-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations]{Appendix
  A1}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10130}{GPTs are GPTs: An
  Early Look at the L\ldots{}} (2023-03-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a2-gpts-are-gpts-an-early-look-at-the-labor-market-impact-potential-of-large-language-models]{Appendix
  A2}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797}{Computing Power
  and the Governance of\ldots{}} (2024-02-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Evidence and Method Note}\label{evidence-and-method-note-9}

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 09 and is
grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source
notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already
normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are
asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are
marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and
speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-9}

This paper asks: How does future if no action is taken function within
the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record
allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the
start: The most credible continuity scenario is not science-fiction
apocalypse. It is a slower regime of deepening dependence, shrinking
cognitive sovereignty, weakened middle-class bargaining power, and
increasingly hierarchical access to machine intelligence. \footnote{Sources:
  U.S. Department of Education / ERIC,
  \href{https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097}{Artificial Intelligence and
  the Futur\ldots{}} (2023-05-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations]{Appendix
  A1}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10130}{GPTs are GPTs: An
  Early Look at the L\ldots{}} (2023-03-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a2-gpts-are-gpts-an-early-look-at-the-labor-market-impact-potential-of-large-language-models]{Appendix
  A2}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797}{Computing Power
  and the Governance of\ldots{}} (2024-02-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A3})} The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show
why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument
about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

\subsection{Historical or Institutional
Context}\label{historical-or-institutional-context-9}

The U.S. Department of Education has already framed AI in learning
environments as a governance issue involving automated decisions, bias,
privacy, and human oversight. Dependency concerns are therefore not
merely cultural panic. \footnote{Sources: U.S. Department of Education /
  ERIC, \href{https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097}{Artificial Intelligence
  and the Futur\ldots{}} (2023-05-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations]{Appendix
  A1})}

\subsection{Main Analysis}\label{main-analysis-9}

The GPTs paper and the IMF note both support an uneven future in which
exposure to generative AI is distributed asymmetrically across
occupations, income positions, and labor systems. The future problem is
not uniform extinction. It is differential vulnerability. \footnote{Sources:
  arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10130}{GPTs are GPTs: An Early
  Look at the L\ldots{}} (2023-03-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a2-gpts-are-gpts-an-early-look-at-the-labor-market-impact-potential-of-large-language-models]{Appendix
  A2}); IMF,
  \href{https://www.imf.org/en/publications/staff-discussion-notes/issues/2024/01/14/gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work-542379}{Gen-AI:
  Artificial Intelligence and t\ldots{}} (2024-01-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a4-gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work]{Appendix
  A4})}

Compute-governance research supports the claim that high-end capability
is likely to remain infrastructure-concentrated unless policy changes
the terms of access. That means unequal access to machine intelligence
is already an infrastructure question, not only a talent question.
\footnote{Sources: arXiv,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797}{Computing Power and the
  Governance of\ldots{}} (2024-02-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A3})}

The continuity scenario also includes unequal fallback. Some of the same
actors asking institutions and publics to normalize deeper dependence on
AI and platform infrastructure have also publicly described or been
credibly reported as building private redundancy, jurisdictional exit,
and survivability options: Altman's 2016 prep disclosures and New
Zealand fallback, Zuckerberg's Kauai shelter complex, and Thiel's long
association with New Zealand retreat logic and elite-exit politics. That
does not prove foreknowledge, coordinated collapse engineering, or a
hidden command chain. It does show how continuity can stratify not only
access to intelligence, but access to backup, redundancy, and escape
when shared systems fail or become politically untrustworthy.
\footnote{Sources: Tad Friend,
  \href{https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2016/10/10/sam-altmans-manifest-destiny}{Sam
  Altman's Manifest Destiny} (2016-10-03; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a5-sam-altman-s-manifest-destiny]{Appendix
  A5}); Makena Kelly,
  \href{https://www.wired.com/story/mark-zuckerberg-inside-hawaii-compound}{Inside
  Mark Zuckerberg's Top-Secret H\ldots{}} (2023-12-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a6-inside-mark-zuckerbergs-top-secret-hawaii-compound]{Appendix
  A6}); Charlotte Phillipp,
  \href{https://people.com/mark-zuckerberg-hawaii-bunker-5000-square-feet-8765376}{Mark
  Zuckerberg Downplays His 5,000-S\ldots{}} (2024-12-20; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a7-mark-zuckerberg-downplays-his-5-000-square-foot-underground-bunker-in-hawaii-just-a-little-shelter]{Appendix
  A7}); Mark O'Connell,
  \href{https://www.theguardian.com/news/2018/feb/15/why-silicon-valley-billionaires-are-prepping-for-the-apocalypse-in-new-zealand}{Why
  Silicon Valley billionaires are p\ldots{}} (2018-02-15; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a8-why-silicon-valley-billionaires-are-prepping-for-the-apocalypse-in-new-zealand]{Appendix
  A8}); Rebecca Rose,
  \href{https://www.ft.com/content/76ca455c-3b16-4413-8738-3f17431d125b}{New
  Zealand woos the wealthy with `go\ldots{}} (2025-09-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a9-new-zealand-woos-the-wealthy-with-golden-visa-changes]{Appendix
  A9})}

The financial and moral-warning layers also matter. The BIS warned of
fragile capex dynamics and weak near-term returns in the AI buildout,
while Dario Amodei publicly warned that AI-era wealth concentration
could strain democratic society. These are not identical claims, but
together they strengthen the dossier's continuity scenario of hierarchy
rather than broad emancipation. \footnote{Sources: Bank for
  International Settlements,
  \href{https://www.bis.org/publ/arpdf/ar2026e.pdf}{Annual Economic
  Report 2026} (2026-06-28; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a10-annual-economic-report-2026]{Appendix
  A10}); Dario Amodei,
  \href{https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology}{The
  Adolescence of Technology} (2026-01-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a11-the-adolescence-of-technology]{Appendix
  A11})}

Interpretation: if current platform habits, labor structures, weights
enclosure, compute chokepoints, and asymmetric fallback systems continue
reinforcing one another, the likely future is not total machine takeover
but a regime of dependency, access hierarchy, and weaker cognitive
sovereignty. A contextual formulation from Dion Wiggins is useful here:
continuity can disguise dependency when systems still appear to function
while effective authority migrates elsewhere. That is precisely why the
dossier's three reforms belong together rather than as isolated policy
tweaks. \footnote{Sources: FTC,
  \href{https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services}{A
  Look Behind the Screens: Examining\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a12-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services]{Appendix
  A12}); Meta,
  \href{https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/}{Mark
  Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp\ldots{}} (2023-03-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a13-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees]{Appendix
  A13}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797}{Computing Power
  and the Governance of\ldots{}} (2024-02-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A3}); Dario Amodei,
  \href{https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology}{The
  Adolescence of Technology} (2026-01-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a11-the-adolescence-of-technology]{Appendix
  A11}); Dion Wiggins,
  \href{https://digitalsovereigntyimperative.com/}{The Digital
  Sovereignty Imperative} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a14-the-digital-sovereignty-imperative]{Appendix
  A14})}

\subsection{Position Within the
Series}\label{position-within-the-series-9}

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest
companions are Chapter 05 (Talent, overhiring, valuation, and layoffs),
Chapter 07 (Imperial control over access), Chapter 08 (Power network,
legitimacy, and institutional capture), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that
series logic, Chapter 09 explains why inaction would compound
dependency, hierarchy, and cognitive enclosure across the whole system.

\subsection{Counterarguments}\label{counterarguments-9}

This chapter must remain explicit that it is a projection built from
documented trend lines, not a prophecy. The evidence is stronger on
labor exposure, governance risk, and access concentration than on
long-run learning outcomes or a single inevitable end state. \footnote{Sources:
  U.S. Department of Education / ERIC,
  \href{https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097}{Artificial Intelligence and
  the Futur\ldots{}} (2023-05-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations]{Appendix
  A1}); IMF,
  \href{https://www.imf.org/en/publications/staff-discussion-notes/issues/2024/01/14/gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work-542379}{Gen-AI:
  Artificial Intelligence and t\ldots{}} (2024-01-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a4-gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work]{Appendix
  A4})}

\subsection{Limits of the Evidence}\label{limits-of-the-evidence-9}

This chapter must remain explicit that it is a projection built from
documented trend lines, not a prophecy. The evidence is stronger on
labor exposure, governance risk, and access concentration than on
long-run learning outcomes or a single inevitable end state. \footnote{Sources:
  U.S. Department of Education / ERIC,
  \href{https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097}{Artificial Intelligence and
  the Futur\ldots{}} (2023-05-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations]{Appendix
  A1}); IMF,
  \href{https://www.imf.org/en/publications/staff-discussion-notes/issues/2024/01/14/gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work-542379}{Gen-AI:
  Artificial Intelligence and t\ldots{}} (2024-01-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a4-gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work]{Appendix
  A4})}

\subsection{Reform Relevance}\label{reform-relevance-9}

If no action is taken, platform immunity, private control over weights,
and opaque state-company entanglement stop looking like separate policy
issues and start looking like one reinforced system. That is why
\texttt{Refactor\ Section\ 230},
\texttt{AI\ Weights\ as\ Patrimony\ of\ Humanity}, and
\texttt{Limit\ Government\ Capture\ by\ AI\ Companies} must be read as
one program of democratic reassertion. \footnote{Sources: FTC,
  \href{https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services}{A
  Look Behind the Screens: Examining\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a12-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services]{Appendix
  A12}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/}{OpenAI's
  proposals for the U.S. AI Ac\ldots{}} (2025-03-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a15-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan]{Appendix
  A15}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797}{Computing Power
  and the Governance of\ldots{}} (2024-02-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion-9}

If no action is taken, platform immunity, private control over weights,
and opaque state-company entanglement stop looking like separate policy
issues and start looking like one reinforced system. The final chapter
therefore has to answer the question the whole dossier implies: what
does resistance look like once the solution space is no longer
open-ended but doctrinally explicit? In the architecture of this series,
Chapter 09 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also
advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

\subsection{Notes}\label{notes-9}

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point
readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local
appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among
\texttt{Documented\ Fact}, \texttt{Disputed\ Fact},
\texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}, and
\texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}. In Chapter 09, those boundaries
remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix
sections that summarize claims and caution points.

\section{Chapter 10. What to do}\label{chapter-10.-what-to-do}

\subsection{Abstract}\label{abstract-10}

The response program is no longer an open-ended menu. The platform
chapters already established that sovereign-scale systems shape
exposure, amplification, targeting, monetization, and moderation
conditions at mass scale. The essential political choice is whether
intelligence infrastructure will remain a privately concentrated empire
built on captured social life, or whether societies will recover
sovereignty over the terms on which cognition itself is organized.

\subsection{Keywords}\label{keywords-10}

What to do; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; Section 230 reform;
AI weights; patrimony of humanity; government capture; evidence-based
dossier

\subsection{Research Question}\label{research-question-10}

What reform doctrine follows from the dossier's evidence on platform
power, frontier-model enclosure, and state-company entanglement?

\subsection{Scope and Framing Note}\label{scope-and-framing-note-10}

This paper examines what to do within the broader dossier \emph{From
Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism}. It is intentionally written
to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence
boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in
paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It
contributes to the series as a paper that synthesizes the entire dossier
into the direct three-reform program. Adjacent reinforcement appears in
Chapter 00, Chapter 01, Chapter 02, Chapter 04, Chapter 07, Chapter 08,
Chapter 09, but those cross-references are supportive rather than
required for basic comprehension.

\subsection{Central Thesis}\label{central-thesis-10}

The response program is no longer an open-ended menu. The dossier now
takes a direct three-part stance:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{Refactor\ Section\ 230}
\item
  \texttt{AI\ Weights\ as\ Patrimony\ of\ Humanity}
\item
  \texttt{Limit\ Government\ Capture\ by\ AI\ Companies}
\end{itemize}

Cognitive literacy, digital hygiene, labor organization, competition
policy, and public-interest infrastructure remain necessary, but they
function as implementation arms of these three reforms rather than as
disconnected alternatives. \footnote{Sources: U.S. Department of
  Education / ERIC, \href{https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097}{Artificial
  Intelligence and the Futur\ldots{}} (2023-05-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations]{Appendix
  A1}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.07700}{In Oxford
  Handbook on AI Governance:\ldots{}} (2021-08-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance]{Appendix
  A2}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05761}{Interactions
  Between Artificial Intel\ldots{}} (2024-12-07; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges]{Appendix
  A3})}

\subsection{Evidence and Method Note}\label{evidence-and-method-note-10}

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 10 and is
grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source
notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already
normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are
asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are
marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and
speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-10}

This paper asks: What reform doctrine follows from the dossier's
evidence on platform power, frontier-model enclosure, and state-company
entanglement? Its working answer is clear from the start: The response
program is no longer an open-ended menu. The dossier now takes a direct
three-part stance:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{Refactor\ Section\ 230}
\item
  \texttt{AI\ Weights\ as\ Patrimony\ of\ Humanity}
\item
  \texttt{Limit\ Government\ Capture\ by\ AI\ Companies}
\end{itemize}

Cognitive literacy, digital hygiene, labor organization, competition
policy, and public-interest infrastructure remain necessary, but they
function as implementation arms of these three reforms rather than as
disconnected alternatives. \footnote{Sources: U.S. Department of
  Education / ERIC, \href{https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097}{Artificial
  Intelligence and the Futur\ldots{}} (2023-05-24; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations]{Appendix
  A1}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.07700}{In Oxford
  Handbook on AI Governance:\ldots{}} (2021-08-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance]{Appendix
  A2}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05761}{Interactions
  Between Artificial Intel\ldots{}} (2024-12-07; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges]{Appendix
  A3})} The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show
why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument
about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

\subsection{Historical or Institutional
Context}\label{historical-or-institutional-context-10}

The institutional context for what to do cannot be treated as an
isolated debate. Each chapter in this series is part of a longer
sequence in which platform design, data capture, labor correction, model
governance, and state access accumulate into a single political
structure. This section therefore identifies the historical or
institutional setting needed for a standalone reader before moving
deeper into the paper's own evidentiary claims.

\subsection{Main Analysis}\label{main-analysis-10}

The platform chapters already established that sovereign-scale systems
shape exposure, amplification, targeting, monetization, and moderation
conditions at mass scale. The training chapters established that
frontier capability is built from collectively produced expression under
contested legal conditions. The access and legitimacy chapters
established that frontier firms already move through policy formation,
strategic access regimes, lobbying, procurement channels, and
public-private legitimacy structures. This final chapter turns those
upstream findings into doctrine. \footnote{Sources: FTC,
  \href{https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services}{A
  Look Behind the Screens: Examining\ldots{}} (n.d.; see

  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a5-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory]{Appendix
  A5}); U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A6}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix

  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude

  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix

  \href{https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation}{Army
  Launches Detachment 201: Executi\ldots{}} (2025-06-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a9-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation]{Appendix
  A9})}

\subsubsection{Refactor Section 230}\label{refactor-section-230}

Documented problem: sovereign-scale platforms do not merely host speech.
They structure exposure, optimize amplification, monetize targeting, and
govern the conditions under which civic, psychological, and political
harm scales. \footnote{Sources: FTC,
  \href{https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services}{A
  Look Behind the Screens: Examining\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services]{Appendix
  A4}); HHS,
  \href{https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html}{Social
  Media and Youth Mental Health:\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a5-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory]{Appendix
  A5}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00299}{When Kids Mode
  Isn't For Kids: Invest\ldots{}} (2025-06-30; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a10-when-kids-mode-isn-t-for-kids-investigating-tiktok-s-under-13-experience]{Appendix
  A10}); Pew Research Center,
  \href{https://www.pewresearch.org/global/2022/12/06/social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier/}{Social
  Media Seen as Mostly Good for\ldots{}} (2022-12-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a11-social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier]{Appendix
  A11})}

Interpretation: the legal principle should shift from passive hosting to
systemic design responsibility. Refactoring Section \texttt{230} does
not require treating every user post as if it were authored by the
platform. It requires treating sovereign-scale platforms as
infrastructures whose reach, design, and monetized amplification create
duties commensurate with their power. \footnote{Sources: FTC,
  \href{https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services}{A
  Look Behind the Screens: Examining\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services]{Appendix
  A4}); HHS,
  \href{https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html}{Social
  Media and Youth Mental Health:\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a5-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory]{Appendix
  A5}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.00590}{Quantifying
  social organization and p\ldots{}} (2020-10-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a12-quantifying-social-organization-and-political-polarization-in-online-platforms]{Appendix
  A12})}

Policy direction: obligations should track reach, recommendation
architecture, amplification, targeting, monetization, moderation
architecture, and measurable systemic harm. Child-safety design duties,
recommendation auditability, and sovereign-scale platform-risk
obligations fit this framework more naturally than either the old
passive-intermediary fiction or blanket publisher liability. \footnote{Sources:
  arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00299}{When Kids Mode Isn't
  For Kids: Invest\ldots{}} (2025-06-30; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a10-when-kids-mode-isn-t-for-kids-investigating-tiktok-s-under-13-experience]{Appendix
  A10}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02176}{Unfiltered: How
  Teens Engage in Body\ldots{}} (2025-04-02; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a13-unfiltered-how-teens-engage-in-body-image-and-shaming-discussions-via-instagram-direct-messages-dms]{Appendix
  A13}); FTC,
  \href{https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services}{A
  Look Behind the Screens: Examining\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services]{Appendix
  A4})}

\subsubsection{AI Weights as Patrimony of
Humanity}\label{ai-weights-as-patrimony-of-humanity}

Documented problem: frontier-model capability is built from humanity's
accumulated language, art, code, labor, science, and socially filtered
judgment under legal conditions that remain actively contested.
\footnote{Sources: U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A6}); GovInfo,
  \href{https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-08-30/pdf/2023-18624.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2023-08-30; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a14-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-notice-of-inquiry]{Appendix
  A14}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf}{Language
  Models are Unsupervised Mult\ldots{}} (2019-02-14; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a15-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners]{Appendix
  A15}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a16-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A16})}

Interpretation: once that structure is clear, frontier weights cannot be
treated as ordinary private property by default. The dossier's strongest
normative direction is patrimony-oriented governance: public-trust
logic, anti-monopoly limits, democratic oversight, interoperability
duties, and restrictions on exclusive commercialization. In its
strongest form, this doctrine pushes toward AI treated more like civic
utility infrastructure: open and as broadly accessible as
public-interest governance, safety constraints, and durable shared
infrastructure can make it. This is advocated doctrine, not settled law.
\footnote{Sources: TwinChat,
  \href{https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee}{AI
  Copyright Weights: A New Frontier\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a17-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law]{Appendix
  A17}); U.S. House of Representatives,
  \href{https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf}{Bipartisan
  House Task Force Report on\ldots{}} (2024-12-17; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a18-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence]{Appendix
  A18}); U.S. Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A6}); U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California via
  CourtListener,
  \href{https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.cand.434709/gov.uscourts.cand.434709.231.0_3.pdf}{Bartz
  et al.~v. Anthropic PBC, Order\ldots{}} (2025-06-23; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a19-bartz-et-al-v-anthropic-pbc-order-on-fair-use]{Appendix
  A19})}

Policy direction: transparency around material training inputs,
anti-enclosure limits for dominant model holders, democratic governance
mechanisms, and restrictions on exclusive commercialization where public
support is material belong inside this reform. \footnote{Sources: U.S.
  Copyright Office,
  \href{https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf}{Copyright
  and Artificial Intelligence\ldots{}} (2025-05-09; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version]{Appendix
  A6}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/}{OpenAI's
  proposals for the U.S. AI Ac\ldots{}} (2025-03-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan]{Appendix
  A20}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708}{Reddit
  strikes \$60M deal allowing Goo\ldots{}} (2024-02-22; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a16-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans]{Appendix
  A16})}

\subsubsection{Limit Government Capture by AI
Companies}\label{limit-government-capture-by-ai-companies}

Documented problem: frontier firms already participate in policy
formation, export-control protection, government-only offerings,
classified-environment distribution channels, lobbying, electoral
vehicles, and military-adjacent personnel structures. \footnote{Sources:
  OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/}{OpenAI's
  proposals for the U.S. AI Ac\ldots{}} (2025-03-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan]{Appendix
  A20}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A7}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude
  Gov models for U.S. national s\ldots{}} (2025-06-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix
  A8}); Palantir Technologies,
  \href{https://investors.palantir.com/news-details/2024/Anthropic-and-Palantir-Partner-to-Bring-Claude-AI-Models-to-AWS-for-U.S.-Government-Intelligence-and-Defense-Operations/}{Anthropic
  and Palantir Partner to Bri\ldots{}} (2024-11-07; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a21-anthropic-and-palantir-partner-to-bring-claude-ai-models-to-aws-for-u-s-government-intelligence-and-defense-operations]{Appendix
  A21}); U.S. Army Public Affairs,
  \href{https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation}{Army
  Launches Detachment 201: Executi\ldots{}} (2025-06-13; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a9-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation]{Appendix
  A9}); Axios,
  \href{https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter}{Anthropic
  outspends OpenAI in biggest\ldots{}} (2026-04-21; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a22-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter]{Appendix
  A22})}

Interpretation: any frontier firm benefiting materially from public
funding, procurement, compute support, export-control protection,
regulatory protection, or national-security access should face
heightened public obligations. Public support should increase
transparency duties rather than deepen private enclosure. This is an
anti-capture doctrine, not a generic appeal to ethics and not a claim of
total hidden control. \footnote{Sources: OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A7}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude
  Gov models for U.S. national s\ldots{}} (2025-06-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix
  A8}); Federal Election Commission,
  \href{https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/}{LEADING THE
  FUTURE - committee overview} (2026-03-31; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a23-leading-the-future-committee-overview]{Appendix
  A23}); Dario Amodei,
  \href{https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology}{The
  Adolescence of Technology} (2026-01-01; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a24-the-adolescence-of-technology]{Appendix
  A24})}

Policy direction: procurement visibility, subsidy transparency, lobbying
disclosure, conflict-of-interest rules, revolving-door limits,
independent audits, model-risk reporting, and limits on exclusive
private benefit from publicly supported AI infrastructure should be the
baseline. Where firms are simultaneously building quasi-public
intelligence infrastructure and private survivability hedges, the burden
for public accountability should rise further: dependency-building
should not be paired with private exemption from shared failure without
stronger oversight and obligations. \footnote{Sources: AP,

\subsection{Position Within the
Series}\label{position-within-the-series-10}

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest
companions are Chapter 00 (Opening framework), Chapter 01 (Social
networks as infrastructure for free data capture), Chapter 02 (Social
erosion and moral reconfiguration), Chapter 04 (AI arrives when the
human raw material already exists), Chapter 07 (Imperial control over
access), Chapter 08 (Power network, legitimacy, and institutional
capture), Chapter 09 (Future if no action is taken). In that series
logic, Chapter 10 synthesizes the entire dossier into the direct
three-reform program.

\subsection{Counterarguments}\label{counterarguments-10}

This chapter is partly normative by design. The current corpus supports
the legitimacy of a three-reform agenda, but not yet a fully
implementation-ready statutory blueprint in every domain. That is not a
weakness if the distinction remains explicit. \footnote{Sources: arXiv,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.07700}{In Oxford Handbook on AI
  Governance:\ldots{}} (2021-08-05; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance]{Appendix
  A2}); arXiv, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05761}{Interactions
  Between Artificial Intel\ldots{}} (2024-12-07; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges]{Appendix
  A3}); AP,
  \href{https://apnews.com/article/67feef411ef311f0be543f546ef34b3d}{FTC
  opens inquiry into Big Tech's par\ldots{}} (2024-01-25; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a25-ftc-opens-inquiry-into-big-tech-s-partnerships-with-leading-ai-startups]{Appendix
  A25})}

\subsection{Reform Relevance}\label{reform-relevance-10}

The essential political choice is whether intelligence infrastructure
will remain a privately concentrated empire built on captured social
life, or whether societies will recover sovereignty over the terms on
which cognition itself is organized. The dossier's answer is explicit:
sovereign-scale platform accountability, patrimony-oriented governance
of frontier weights, and strong limits on opaque state-corporate
capture. \footnote{Sources: FTC,
  \href{https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services}{A
  Look Behind the Screens: Examining\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services]{Appendix
  A4}); TwinChat,
  \href{https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee}{AI
  Copyright Weights: A New Frontier\ldots{}} (n.d.; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a17-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law]{Appendix
  A17}); OpenAI,
  \href{https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/}{Introducing
  OpenAI for Government} (2025-06-16; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government]{Appendix
  A7}); Anthropic,
  \href{https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers}{Claude
  Gov models for U.S. national s\ldots{}} (2025-06-06; see
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers]{Appendix
  A8})}

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion-10}

The essential political choice is whether intelligence infrastructure
will remain a privately concentrated empire built on captured social
life, or whether societies will recover sovereignty over the terms on
which cognition itself is organized. The dossier closes here. In the
architecture of this series, Chapter 10 therefore functions as a
self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward
the dossier's three reforms.

\subsection{Notes}\label{notes-10}

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point
readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local
appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among
\texttt{Documented\ Fact}, \texttt{Disputed\ Fact},
\texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}, and
\texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}. In Chapter 10, those boundaries
remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix
sections that summarize claims and caution points.

\subsection{Appendix E. Evidence
Boundaries}\label{appendix-e.-evidence-boundaries-10}

\subsubsection{Appendix E1. Disputed
Matters}\label{ch-10-appendix-e1-disputed-matters}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b38-whether-existing-copyright-and-fair-use-doctrine-can-absorb-frontier-model-traini]{Appendix
  B38}: That whether existing copyright and fair-use doctrine can absorb
  frontier-model training without consent or compensation remains
  actively contested across agencies, courts, and litigants.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Appendix E2. Interpretive
Boundaries}\label{ch-10-appendix-e2-interpretive-boundaries}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b39-sovereign-scale-platforms-should-be-regulated-on-the-basis-of-design-amplificati]{Appendix
  B39}: That sovereign-scale platforms should be regulated on the basis
  of design, amplification, targeting, monetization, moderation
  architecture, and systemic reach rather than passive-hosting logic.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b40-documented-youth-exposure-recommendation-amplification-trust-erosion-and-fragm]{Appendix
  B40}: That documented youth exposure, recommendation amplification,
  trust erosion, and fragmentation support a sovereign-scale duty
  framework under a refactored Section 230 focused on design, exposure,
  and systemic harm.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b41-frontier-ai-weights-can-be-read-as-condensed-claims-over-collectively-produced-hu]{Appendix
  B41}: That frontier AI weights can be read as condensed claims over
  collectively produced human expression and therefore justify
  patrimony-oriented governance.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b42-current-official-records-show-frontier-labs-and-adjacent-platform-firms-building]{Appendix
  B42}: That current official records show frontier labs and adjacent
  platform firms building formal government-facing channels through
  secure offerings, classified-environment access, accredited cloud
  stacks, and even military personnel embedding rather than engaging the
  state only through lobbying or abstract policy rhetoric.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b43-firms-benefiting-from-export-control-protection-strategic-state-access-or-privi]{Appendix
  B43}: That firms benefiting from export-control protection, strategic
  state access, or privileged public coordination should face stronger
  transparency and public-obligation duties than ordinary software
  vendors.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b44-frontier-legitimacy-is-increasingly-stabilized-through-a-combination-of-mission-r]{Appendix
  B44}: That frontier legitimacy is increasingly stabilized through a
  combination of mission rhetoric, elite board composition, security
  credentials, policy access, and political spending.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b45-the-legitimacy-field-around-frontier-ai-is-now-reinforced-not-only-by-boards-pol]{Appendix
  B45}: That the legitimacy field around frontier AI is now reinforced
  not only by boards, policy essays, and lobbying, but by operational
  state interfaces that normalize privileged access and public-private
  dependence.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b46-the-strongest-bounded-reading-of-elite-fallback-architecture-is-a-revealed-prefer]{Appendix
  B46}: That the strongest bounded reading of elite fallback
  architecture is a revealed-preference gap between public stewardship
  language and private survivability hedging.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b47-the-continuity-scenario-includes-asymmetry-of-fallback-and-exit-with-elite-actor]{Appendix
  B47}: That the continuity scenario includes asymmetry of fallback and
  exit, with elite actors purchasing redundancy while ordinary users are
  asked to normalize dependence.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b48-if-current-platform-habits-labor-structures-and-compute-chokepoints-continue-re]{Appendix
  B48}: That if current platform habits, labor structures, and compute
  chokepoints continue reinforcing one another, the most credible
  continuity scenario is dependency, access hierarchy, and weaker
  cognitive sovereignty.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b49-frontier-firms-receiving-material-public-support-state-coordination-export-cont]{Appendix
  B49}: That frontier firms receiving material public support, state
  coordination, export-control protection, or national-security access
  should face heightened transparency, audit, conflict-of-interest,
  procurement-visibility, and exclusive-benefit limits.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b50-sovereign-scale-platforms-should-face-a-section-230-framework-keyed-to-systemic-r]{Appendix
  B50}: That sovereign-scale platforms should face a Section 230
  framework keyed to systemic reach, design, amplification, targeting,
  monetization, moderation architecture, and measurable harm rather than
  passive-hosting logic alone.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b51-cognitive-literacy-labor-organization-competition-policy-and-public-interest-i]{Appendix
  B51}: That cognitive literacy, labor organization, competition policy,
  and public-interest infrastructure should function as implementation
  arms of the dossier's three reforms rather than as disconnected
  options.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b52-frontier-ai-weights-trained-on-collectively-produced-human-expression-justify-a-p]{Appendix
  B52}: That frontier AI weights trained on collectively produced human
  expression justify a patrimony-oriented governance response including
  public-trust logic, anti-monopoly limits, democratic oversight, and
  restrictions on exclusive commercialization.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Appendix E3. Speculative Narrative
Risks}\label{ch-10-appendix-e3-speculative-narrative-risks}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b53-refactoring-section-230-for-sovereign-scale-platforms-requires-treating-platforms]{Appendix
  B53}: That refactoring Section 230 for sovereign-scale platforms
  requires treating platforms as ordinary publishers for every user
  utterance regardless of design, amplification, targeting, or
  monetization.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b54-current-legal-uncertainty-or-congressional-uptake-already-proves-frontier-ai-weig]{Appendix
  B54}: That current legal uncertainty or congressional uptake already
  proves frontier AI weights are legally settled as patrimony, public
  trust property, or common heritage.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b55-bunker-ownership-survival-planning-or-retreat-architecture-prove-elite-foreknow]{Appendix
  B55}: That bunker ownership, survival planning, or retreat
  architecture prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or that
  frontier firms are knowingly engineering it.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b56-founder-philanthropy-or-public-warnings-about-concentration-prove-elite-foreknowl]{Appendix
  B56}: That founder philanthropy or public warnings about concentration
  prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or absolve frontier
  firms of responsibility.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b57-formal-government-only-model-channels-classified-distribution-stacks-or-militar]{Appendix
  B57}: That formal government-only model channels, classified
  distribution stacks, or military personnel embedding by themselves
  prove unlawful capture or total hidden state control over frontier AI
  firms.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b58-the-continuity-scenario-is-a-fixed-prophecy-of-inevitable-collapse-rather-than-a]{Appendix
  B58}: That the continuity scenario is a fixed prophecy of inevitable
  collapse rather than a political trajectory that could still be
  altered by institutional reform.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b59-individual-digital-hygiene-consumer-choice-or-personal-restraint-alone-can-solv]{Appendix
  B59}: That individual digital hygiene, consumer choice, or personal
  restraint alone can solve sovereign-scale platform harm, enclosure of
  frontier weights, or opaque state-company entanglement without
  structural reform.
\item
  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-b60-voluntary-elite-philanthropy-or-self-limitation-can-substitute-for-structural-ref]{Appendix
  B60}: That voluntary elite philanthropy or self-limitation can
  substitute for structural reform of platform power, frontier-weight
  ownership, and state-company capture.
\end{itemize}

  \hyperref[ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges]{Appendix
  A3}. Companion note: Note N-103. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That a
